## Summary

- **599 normative items** (RENDERING-001 – RENDERING-599). By kind: rule 260, decision 65, budget 62, test 43, interface 41, acceptance-bar 40, algorithm-step 16, defined-term 15, evidence 14, number 14, mechanism 8, history 8, type 7, summary 4, invariant 2.
- **Subject owner:** this part 488 (§8 263, §9 60, §11 59, §12 106); another part 42; judgment rationale to move to `docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md` 61; history to drop from the normative text 8.
- **Items whose subject belongs to another part, by part:** determinism 16, pipeline 11, model 8, scope 3, kernel 2, packages 1, clean-room 1. These become references in this part and live in the named section.
- **Items stated in one of this part's sections but owned by another of its sections** (single-source targets inside the part): 11 — 037, 116, 203, 205, 206, 232, 262, 263, 318, 358, 431; mostly §12 budgets restated in §8, §9 and §11 (display-list size, instance budgets, placement budget, SVG export time), and export facts stated in §8.7/§8.9.
- **8 internal conflicts** (4 CONFIRMED, meaning the texts disagree as written; 4 PLAUSIBLE, meaning a reading of the text). Three of the CONFIRMED ones are the still-open round-5 minors NR5-1, NR5-2 and NR5-3, which this pass is allowed to fix; the fourth (bench/calibrate 8 vs 32 tiles) is new.
- **Review markers:** 34 per-section `> PENDING FABLE REVIEW` lines in this part (§8 11, §9 7, §11 6, §12 10), all to be replaced by the single banner.
- **Heaviest restatement clusters (compression targets):**
  - the canvas budget — full statement in §8.8 (3P + 7 MB, 4.25P + 7 MB, tiers 72/128/192 MB, DPR caps, worked cases), repeated in §12.8's table and again in §12.8's canvas paragraph; P = 35 also in §12.5;
  - the two declared misses (first map above ≈ 14 200 buildings; O3 (d) above ≈ 4 500/17 700/58 800 people) — §12 intro, §12.3, §12.5, §12.9 and §8.5's first-paint rationale, with every figure repeated;
  - the SVG budgets (≤ 650 B/building, ≤ 64 B/glyph, ≤ 0.5 KB/distinct glyph, ≤ 0.5 MB/face, ≤ 60 000 elements, Inkscape 120 s) — §11.1's table row and §12.7 nearly verbatim;
  - the export transient list — §8.1 item 6, §8.9 threads (1) and (3), §8.9's crossing row, §11.1 'Where exports run', §12.7 PNG and §12.8 export peak (≈ 1.9 KB/building in three places);
  - 'the tile worker holds no model' — §8.1 item 1, §8.6, §8.9 (3) and its rationale, §12.8;
  - the light step (15°/5°) and the lit crown bake — §8.2, §8.4 (definition and light pass), §8.8 (tile key, canvas budget), §8.9 (queue class 3), §8.10 (twice), §12.7;
  - placement stepping — the unit list and its rationale appear in both §8.9 and §9.2; step(8)/step(4) in §8.9 three times and in §12.7;

### Cross-part observations (not internal conflicts)

- §11.2 l.8600-8602 says a host without WebAssembly receives `E_ENGINE_UNAVAILABLE` under the kernel's Clipper2 fallback — the code KERNEL-C02 finds missing from §3.1's KernelError union. Reconcile with the kernel lane.
- `LayerSpec` is declared once, in §8.4 (grep: no other declaration); its wording '`LayerSpec` gains an occlusion policy' is history and should go. `LabelEntity` and `QuarterNode` are declared only in §9.1. §14 (l.10124, l.10130) carries copies of `LabelPlacer` and `TileRasterOpts` ('§14 declares the same'), so NR5-1 and NR5-2 must be fixed in both places, or §14 should point to §8.9/§9.2 instead of copying.
- §12.4's column header says 'mechanism the budget assumes (§4 follows it)', making §12.4 the authority for S1–S14 mechanisms (grids, lattice, flood, blur). Single-source needs one direction: §4 owns mechanisms, §12.4 keeps work counts and p50 only.
- §12.2's size ladder (bands, targets, buildings, frames) overlaps §1.1's rung table; the frame geometry (aspect 0.5–2, 2²³-cm range) is kernel §3.2 (9)/(1). The kernel inventory already notes '8 × 8 km' holds only at aspect 1; §12.2's header states the aspect rule, so no conflict inside this part.
- Restated from other parts (become pointers): the quarter merge rule (§9.4 ← §4.5); naming, uniqueness and renames (§9.6 ← §10.4, §6.3); load verification and the bridged keep (§11.3, §12.6, §12.8 ← §2.9 steps 2–4); replay order (§8.1 item 4, §12.6 ← §7.3); permalinks (§11.1 ← §6.4); fate confirmation (§11.2 ← §7.3 item 4); `logHead` (§11.3 ← §2.9); spacing ranges 300–500 / 400–600 cm (§8.10 ← §4.9); stream-key compositions (§8.2 random style, §8.10 stamp tiles and crowns, §9.5 compass and emblem ← §6.1); the instance edit event (§8.10 ← §7.1's event union); heights (§8.4 ← §2.6).
- Checked and consistent (arithmetic re-done here): every §12.3 law at 1 333 / 5 200 / 13 460 buildings and at every band end, the O3 (d) coverage table, the max-clause bounds and the people conversions at 3.4 per building; every §12.5 first-map law (model law + F(B)) and its table values, knees 14 190 / 11 400; §12.4's critical path sums to 255 ms and the single-worker path to ≈ 334 ms; P = 35 / 24 / 20 / 12 and the canvas needs 112 / 156 / 67 / 43 / 47 MB; §12.8's CITY line (≈ 76 + 34; ≈ 91 + 19 MB) and export peaks (≈ 1.9 KB × 1 333 / 5 200 / 13 460 / 35 000); §12.9's MFCG and FTG comparisons; the MarkTable 54-B and LabelTable ≤ 96-B / InstanceTable ≤ 40-B row sums; the symbolisation widths at 0.816 px/m; §11.1's ≈ 15.7 km and ≈ 21 / ≈ 54 ms; the .dd2vtt ≈ 123 m; §8.9's ≤ 16 / ≤ 24 ms export share and 16 furniture boxes.

Columns: id · kind · statement (our words, with values) · values · stated at · restated at (inside this part) · subject part → canonical home · notes. `rendering (§n)` = this part; `other: …` = another part's subject; `rationale` = move to `docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md`; `history` = drop (the validation record keeps it). | 028 | rule | Way casings (slot 5) and way bodies (slot 6) are separate slots holding every way class; shipped sheets' entries {slot 5, 'way.*', passes [1]} and {slot 6, 'way.*', passes [2]}, so every way casing precedes every way body across the scene (GEO-28); masses, fields, woods and shadows keep their slots after the roads (K16, NG shadow order, MFCG wards after roads). | slots 5, 6 | §8.1 item 3 l.6866-6870 | §8.2 layer-order table | rendering (§8) → §8.2 layer-order table |  |
| 029 | mechanism | Incremental update: after an edit or scoped regeneration the builder gets the changed handles (§7.3 locality closure) and emits a DisplayDelta: removed marks, new marks for changed entities and dependants (a way's marks when a junction moved; a district's ground mark when a block changed), their label and instance rows, and dirty boxes = old and new mark bboxes dilated by the largest cross-tile effect (§8.8). Cost O(changed marks). | O(changed marks) | §8.1 item 4 l.6872-6876 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.1 item 4 |  |
| 030 | rule | During a replay (loading a log, undo beyond scoped records, migration) the display-list builder is suppressed and runs once at the end (SCL-9); S13a is model data and runs at each event's log position (§7.3 item 2); only S14 and S13b wait for the end. | — | §8.1 item 4 l.6876-6878 | §7.3 item 2 | other: determinism §7.3 → §7.3 item 2 | Replay ordering is editing's; §8.1 should point. |
| 031 | rule | Every derived artefact (roof geometry, label spine, footprint mass, instance set, tile raster, glyph run) is keyed by (entity key or tile address, content revision, band, style version, roof construction where it applies), never by id alone (LC-23, NF-D10). | — | §8.1 item 5 l.6879-6882 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.1 item 5 |  |
| 032 | rule | Every hash drawn at render time (multi-colour role index, weathering, stamp tile variant, grid jitter, crown index/colour/scale jitter, hatch phase, roof detail, ruin dash) is RH32(tag, key, salt[, ordinal or cell][, style id]); the style id enters only for looks (colours, crowns, hatch), never positions. | — | §8.1 item 5 l.6882-6887 | §8.2 multi-colour pick; §8.10 | rendering (§8) → §8.1 item 5 |  |
| 033 | defined-term | Render key = low 64 bits (words 0 and 1) of the §6.1 entity key (seed key mixed with lineage hash, layer and island), cached in the arena beside the lineage hash (VD2-6). | 64 bits | §8.1 item 5 l.6885-6887 | §8.1 MarkTable.key l.6805; §8.1 rationale l.6910-6917 | other: determinism §6.1 → §6.1 (entity key); §8.1 (render key use) | Entity-key composition is §6.1's; §8.1 should say 'low 64 bits of §6.1's entity key' once. |
| 034 | defined-term | RH32 = the render hash, distinct from the string hash H32 of §13.6; the tag is an index into a fixed render-tag table; words folded through MurmurHash3's 32-bit finaliser; never reads the interned handle. | — | §8.1 item 5 l.6887-6890 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.1 item 5 |  |
| 035 | rule | Because the entity key is a pure function of model data (seed and lineage), an unchanged model renders pixel-identically after a reload (§6.2) and golden PNGs hold (§13.3); where a render rule orders entities (tint colouring, §8.2) it uses address order (§6.3). | — | §8.1 item 5 l.6890-6893 | §8.2 tints | rendering (§8) → §8.1 item 5 |  |
| 036 | rule | Regenerate (keep settings) re-mints the same lineage and look; New random gives a successor a fresh lineage (§6.3) and look; another seed (or Enter's seed + 1) changes every entity key, so coinciding derivation trees draw different variants, jitters, species, crowns and colours (VD2-6). | — | §8.1 item 5 l.6893-6897 | — | other: determinism §6.3 → §6.3 | Consequence of §6.1/§6.3 lineage rules; pointer only. |
| 037 | budget | Display-list size: 54 B/mark fixed + 4 B/mark per attribute column + label/instance tables + geometry; CITY city band ≈ 60 000 marks ≈ 3.2 MB fixed, ≈ 2.4 MB attributes, ≈ 4 MB coordinates, ≤ 0.5 MB label/instance/string rows ≈ 10 MB (N-9 c). | ≈ 60 000 marks; ≈ 10 MB | §8.1 item 6 l.6898-6900 | §12.8 memory | rendering (§12) → §12.8 | Memory figure; §12.8 is the budget home. |
| 038 | rule | The tile worker holds the only full display-list copy; after each transfer the generation worker keeps only the columns update() reads (entity, bbox, group). | 24 B/mark, ≈ 1.4 MB at CITY | §8.1 item 6 l.6900-6902 | §8.9; §12.8 | rendering (§8) → §8.9 |  |
| 039 | rule | An export that draws (SVG, PNG, print, .dd2vtt image) builds its own transient list in the generation worker: SVG writes from it there and releases it; a raster export transfers it to the tile worker with its request, which rasters from it and releases it (NR-5, NR3-6). | — | §8.1 item 6 l.6902-6905 | §8.9; §11.1; §12.8 | rendering (§8) → §8.9 |  |
| 040 | decision | Chose a columnar transferable mark table over Mark[] objects: a 60 000-object list costs ≈ 140 ms to deserialise (redteam-scale §7.3) while typed arrays transfer in constant time and slice into deltas. | ≈ 140 ms per 60 000 objects | §8.1 l.6907-6909 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 041 | decision | Chose world-aligned tile rasters over per-district rasters: district boundaries are streets (GEO-28) and a district bitmap cannot follow continuous zoom (SCL-7). | — | §8.1 l.6909-6910 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 042 | decision | Chose the entity key's low 64 bits as the one render-time key over the bare lineage hash (no seed → repeated looks, VD2-6), the address (New random must re-roll look) and the interned handle (session-local, NF-D10); 64 of 128 bits because RH32 folds 32-bit words and two words make an accidental repeat negligible (≈ 3 × 10⁻¹⁰ per 10⁵ entities, §6.1) at 8 B per mark. | 64 bits; ≈ 3×10⁻¹⁰ per 10⁵ entities | §8.1 l.6910-6917 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 043 | decision | Chose slot-major order with passes inside each slot over pass-major (which drew every road casing over masses, fields, woods and shadows, against N-7), keeping GEO-28 through separate casing and body slots. | — | §8.1 l.6917-6920 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |

| 056 | rule | The random-style draw comes from the style.random stream (§6.1) with the setStyle event's seq as creation index and the attempt (0-2) as attempt component; it is a style-time draw written into the event as row operations, so a replay never re-draws it. | attempt 0-2 | §8.2 l.6990-6992 | — | other: determinism §6.1 → §6.1 (stream key); §8.2 (use) | Stream-key composition is determinism's. |
| 057 | algorithm-step | Bounded palette repair: take the first failing pair in matrix row order; raise its lighter role's L* by 1 (up to 97) or, if already 97, lower the darker role's L* by 1 (down to 3); re-check every pair; stop when all pass or after 100 steps; a repaired palette must still pass after sRGB conversion; a failed repair is redrawn on the next attempt; after three failed attempts the family's shipped palette is used and the report says so; ends by construction (≤ 300 steps). | step 1 L*; bounds 97 / 3; 100 steps; 3 attempts; ≤ 300 steps | §8.2 l.6993-6997 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.2 |  |
| 058 | history | The old rule (raise the lighter role until every pair passes) could cycle or never end. | — | §8.2 l.6997-6999 | §8.2 l.7003-7005 | history (validation record; drop from normative text) → validation record |  |
| 059 | evidence | Measured on 2 000 seeded draws per family (fixall_rendering_r2_palette.out): 4-18 % pass as drawn, 77-83 % after first repair (median 5-17 steps), 99.1-99.6 % within three attempts; shipped-palette fallback 0.4-0.9 %. | 2 000 draws; 4-18 %; 77-83 %; 99.1-99.6 %; 0.4-0.9 % | §8.2 l.6999-7001 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.2 | Keep as short pointer. |

| 086 | rule | tint (K12): per district a hue from a greedy colouring of the district adjacency graph in address order (§6.3): hue = base + 137.5° × colour index, stepped by 30° until every neighbour differs by ≥ 30°; method spectrum \| brightness \| overlay; strength 0-1; applied to roofs of built districts; outlines take lerp(ink, tint, 0.3); recomputed when the palette changes; off in ink by default. | 137.5°; 30° steps; ≥ 30°; 0.3 | §8.2 l.7101-7105 | §8.1 item 5 l.6892 | rendering (§8) → §8.2 |  |

| 088 | rule | Materials, season, weather (COV-FTG-F20, K06): override rows may key roof and wall colours by roof.material and the enceinte's wall material; view state (§11.3) carries season ∈ {spring, summer, autumn, winter} and weather ∈ {clear, overcast, snow}; autumn/winter re-skin deciduous crowns (by leafType, deciduous when absent) via role variants (tree@autumn; winter = bare-crown symbol); snow lays the snow role over ground, fields and roofs while road keeps its value; overcast turns projected shadows off (raised under-copy stays); nothing in the model changes, model hash untouched. | 4 seasons; 3 weathers | §8.2 l.7108-7113 | §11.3 view state; §8.8 view-state switches | rendering (§8) → §8.2 |  |
| 089 | rule | Post operations (K07): applied once per tile after its marks: greyscale, sepia, tint(colour, strength), vignette (on the view), pixelate(cellPx), dotScreen(cellPx, angle), scanline(periodPx, alpha), crosshatchNoise(periodPx, alpha); Canvas2D on the tile's pixels in the tile worker; PNG export applies them per tile; SVG carries greyscale, sepia and tint as colour-matrix filters and omits pixel operations, which the export report lists; replaces the 'WebGL2 post hooks' pointer. | ≈ 1-2 ms per 512² tile | §8.2 l.7115-7120 | §11.1 SVG/PNG; §12 per-tile budgets | rendering (§8) → §8.2 |  |

Refused when a pair has no candidate, two candidates tie at highest specificity, or one derived kind is placed in two slots of a band; result cached per style version. | — | §8.2 l.7176-7183 | §8.1 item 3 l.6860-6862 | rendering (§8) → §8.2 |  |

| 156 | rule | Island placement (over water, fields or outskirts by default; over the built core only by assignment) is decided by §4.2 S1 (2), SkyIsland.placement (§2.7) and II.6's placement rule (N-9 d); B-ZOOM reports the ground area occluded by sky layers; the island generator is owner-staged and these fields are its hook. | — | §8.4 l.7299-7303 | §4.2 S1 (2); §2.7; II.6 | other: pipeline §4.2 / II.6 → §4.2 S1 (2) / II.6 | Placement is pipeline's; §8.4 keeps only the occlusion report. |

| 158 | defined-term | Projected shadows (ShadowPass(layer, sun)) are height-derived and OFF by default (owner: shadows optional). | OFF by default | §8.4 l.7306-7307 | §8.2 layer order slot 10 (off by default) | rendering (§8) → §8.4 |  |
| 159 | rule | Shadow casters (WC5): buildings and wings (prism from baseCm to eave and ridge), towers and gatehouses (prism), curtain walls (prism of the band), bridges and piers (deck prism over water), trees (crown offset by crown height), sky layers (island outline projected by elevationCm); a wing's foundation skirt (§2.6) is part of its prism. | — | §8.4 l.7308-7313 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.4 |  |
| 160 | rule | Every caster projects along the sun vector from its base elevation (baseCm, FTG-13) onto the ground; the ground under a footprint lies within FTG-13's plinth tolerance, so a flat projection from the base is exact to that tolerance. | — | §8.4 l.7310-7313 | §2.6 plinth invariant | rendering (§8) → §8.4 |  |
| 161 | rule | Shadows are unioned per layer and composited once per layer per tile — into the base raster in fixed sun mode, and into a per-tile shadow layer of their own in path mode where the tier's canvas budget admits it (§8.8), so a moving sun re-rasters only shadows. | — | §8.4 l.7313-7315 | §8.8 tile layers | rendering (§8) → §8.4 |  |
| 162 | rule | drop mode offsets each caster's footprint by height × cot(altitude) along the sun's azimuth; prism mode draws the swept prism; both from the same heights. | — | §8.4 l.7315-7316 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.4 |  |
| 163 | interface | SunSpec = { azimuthCdeg; altitudeCdeg; colour; intensity; mode 'fixed'\|'path'; latitudeDeg (path mode; default 50) } (K05). | latitude default 50 | §8.4 l.7317 | §14 | rendering (§8) → §8.4 |  |
| 164 | number | Fixed sun by default: azimuth 315°, altitude 45° (light from the upper left). | 315°; 45° | §8.4 l.7318 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.4 |  |
| 165 | rule | In path mode azimuth and altitude come from sunPath(latitudeDeg, dayOfYear, hour) (solar declination and hour angle, evaluated with dmath), driven by the view-state time (§11.3; the simulation module's clock when it runs); the hemisphere follows the latitude's sign. | — | §8.4 l.7318-7320 | §11.3 | rendering (§8) → §8.4 |  |
| 166 | rule | The shadow pass reads the path-mode sun quantised to 1° in azimuth and altitude (§8.8, N-8); every other program that reads the sun — faceLight, the two-tone roof, hillshade and the lit crown bake — reads its light step: the path-mode sun quantised to 15° azimuth and 5° altitude (NR4-5). | 1°; 15° / 5° | §8.4 l.7321-7323 | §8.2 l.7215-7216; §8.8; §8.10 | rendering (§8) → §8.4 | conflict C02 |
| 167 | rule | Below −6° sun altitude the sheet switches to its night variant (night roles; lights glow instances from the module); shadow length is capped at 8 × height near sunrise and sunset; shadows are vector so there is no shadow-resolution setting. | −6°; 8 × height | §8.4 l.7324-7325 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.4 |  |
| 168 | budget | The projected shadow pass costs ≤ 20 % of a tile's raster time (vector offsets and one union per layer) (AM-3); penumbraPx is two vector rings, not a blur. | ≤ 20 % of tile raster time | §8.4 l.7326-7328 | §12 (tile budgets); §8.2 soft shadows | rendering (§8) → §8.4 | Budget may belong in §12; keep one home. |
| 169 | acceptance-bar | Measurement: M2b times the projected pass on METROPOLIS tiles with prism shadows on; pre-decided fallback: a rung that misses the budget draws drop mode instead of prism, and the report says so. | — | §8.4 l.7328-7330 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.4 |  |
| 170 | budget | Moving sun (N-8): in path mode one 1° step re-rasters the visible tiles' shadow layers and re-composes them — ≤ 20 % of the view's settle raster plus ≈ 0.2 ms per tile, inside §12.5's settle budget; a newer step cancels an older step's pending tiles (latest wins); on a tier with no room for shadow layers the step re-rasters base tiles within the same settle budget. | ≤ 20 % settle + ≈ 0.2 ms/tile | §8.4 l.7330-7333 | §12.5 | rendering (§8) → §8.4 |  |
| 171 | acceptance-bar | Measurement: M2b times sun steps on METROPOLIS views, one step per frame, on reference and low-end devices; pre-decided fallback: a tier whose step misses the settle budget quantises the path sun to 3° instead of 1°, and the report says so. | 3° fallback | §8.4 l.7333-7336 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.4 |  |
| 172 | mechanism | Light pass (NR4-5): when the path sun enters a new light step, a light pass first bakes the lit crown sprites for it (§8.10), then re-rasters the visible tiles' under and over layers (or base layers on a tier without shadow layers) at that step, centre-out like a settle, each tile swapped in when ready. | ≤ 150 ms CITY; ≤ 250 ms METROPOLIS (§12.5) | §8.4 l.7336-7339 | §12.5; §8.10 | rendering (§8) → §8.4 |  |
| 173 | rule | A light pass is never cancelled by a newer light step: the newest waits and starts its own pass when the running one completes, skipping steps between, so the view reaches one light step within two settles; 1° shadow steps go on beside it (latest wins). | within two settles | §8.4 l.7339-7342 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.4 |  |
| 174 | defined-term | Drawn light step = the running light pass's step, or the last completed pass's; every raster of the view uses it, so a tile newly exposed during a pass is rastered at the pass's step; once a pass completes no tile keeps another light step, and tiers with and without shadow layers draw the same roof and crown tones (§8.3 light-step row). | — | §8.4 l.7342-7345 | §8.3 light-step test; §8.8 queue | rendering (§8) → §8.4 | conflict C02 |
| 175 | acceptance-bar | Measurement (M2b, moving-sun runs): every light pass within the settle bound and the crown bake ≤ 20 ms per light step (§12.7); pre-decided fallback: in path mode faceLight, two-tone roofs, hillshade and crowns hold the sheet's fixed sun (only shadows follow the path), reported. | crown bake ≤ 20 ms per light step | §8.4 l.7345-7348 | §12.7; §8.10 | rendering (§8) → §8.4 |  |
| 176 | decision | Chose quantised light steps (15°/5°) for lit programs over the 1° sun (else every 1° step re-rasters under and over — a full settle per frame), and over holding the fixed sun as primary (shadows swinging beside unmoving roof light contradict each other; the fallback accepts that, reported). | — | §8.4 l.7348-7352 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 177 | decision | Chose to let a light pass finish over latest-wins cancellation (a light pass is a whole settle; an animated sun would cancel it before completion, leaving tiles of several light steps). | — | §8.4 l.7352-7354 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 178 | rule | Heights come from §2.6 for every building, wing, tower and roof, so shadows, glTF and CityJSON agree (O17 g). | — | §8.4 l.7355 | §11.1 glTF/CityJSON | other: model §2.6 → §2.6 | Pointer only. |

| 179 | rule | city band content: water; block ground; footprint masses and landmarks; tier-1/2 ways cased and access-critical lanes; symbolised walls, towers and gates; bridges; parks and greens ≥ 0.3 ha in green; wood fills; fields; quarter labels (city cut of the quarter tree, §4.5, §9.4), water, major landmarks, gates. | greens ≥ 0.3 ha | §8.5 l.7361-7363 | §8.5 figure-ground item 1; §9.4 band label sets | rendering (§8) → §8.5 |  |

| 181 | defined-term | Way tiers: tier 1 = through, primary; tier 2 = secondary, quay; tier 3 = lane, cul_de_sac, alley, track, path; pier, bridge and ford draw with their way. | 3 tiers | §8.5 l.7367-7368 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.5 |  |
| 182 | rule | Continuous zoom selects the band with hysteresis (a band is entered at its threshold stop and left one stop past it); added detail cross-fades across that interval; per-band detail budgets by hardware tier; no feature pops more than once across a zoom sweep (B-ZOOM). | one stop hysteresis | §8.5 l.7368-7370 | §8.2 presets (cross-fade); §8.8 zoom stops | rendering (§8) → §8.5 |  |
| 183 | rule | Figure-ground contract (MFP-1) (1): every L2 built block is filled with ground at every band; at the city band yards are not subdivided (parks and greens ≥ 0.3 ha show in green; enclosed courts stay ground at city band and take court from the district band — keeps ink's eight city-band tones, §8.2, N-25), so inside the built area streets and squares are the only road-role voids. | ≥ 0.3 ha | §8.5 l.7374-7377 | §8.2 l.7030-7031 (courts as ground); §8.2 contrast court rows | rendering (§8) → §8.5 |  |
| 184 | algorithm-step | Figure-ground (2): at the city band footprints are drawn as masses: per block, closed across gaps narrower than 1.5 px at the current stop (offset +r then −r, r = 0.75 px × metres per px, in the kernel's integer offset, computed and cached per (block, stop, revision) in the tile worker from footprint marks carrying their block in the block attribute column), never across the corridor of a way drawn at the band (closing clipped by those corridors), filled with building and outlined at the band's outline width; party lines between a mass's footprints at one third of that width only where the footprint's short side ≥ 4 px. | 1.5 px; r = 0.75 px × m/px; ⅓ width; ≥ 4 px | §8.5 l.7378-7384 | §12 (mass closing cost) | rendering (§8) → §8.5 |  |
| 185 | rule | Figure-ground (3): a mass smaller than (2 px)² that merged with nothing is drawn as a 2-px settlement dot in building with an ink rim — a symbol at the centroid, never an enlarged footprint (2-px floor of O12 a). | (2 px)²; 2-px dot | §8.5 l.7385-7386 | §8.2 min feature row | rendering (§8) → §8.5 |  |
| 186 | acceptance-bar | Street-edge recoverability (B-ZOOM, city band, default view, every rung): share of intramural street length (sampled every 2 px along corridor centrelines) with a road/non-road transition within (half-width + 1 px) on both sides ≥ MFCG's value on its size-40/80/130 plain sweep renders less 0.03; measured research-side at M-1 by this metric (§13.5 ⟨M-1⟩) and cross-checked against the product's implementation on M1's golden renders within 0.01. | sample every 2 px; half-width + 1 px; − 0.03; within 0.01 | §8.5 l.7389-7393 | §13.5 | rendering (§8) → §8.5 | Sentence structure garbles 'less 0.03' after the cross-check clause; compress. |
| 187 | rule | Road/ground contrast comes from the matrix (§8.2); gross footprint cover inside the walls is reported next to MFCG's 0.60-0.64 (sizes 40-130) as information, not a target (yards are the owner's plot-model ruling). | 0.60-0.64 | §8.5 l.7393-7394 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.5 |  |
| 188 | acceptance-bar | Pre-decided fallback if recoverability fails at M1: the ink city band adds kerb hairlines (0.25 px, ink) along every open frontage run ≥ 6 m (detached, compound, hovel, vacant and green frontages) and the metric is re-run. | 0.25 px; ≥ 6 m | §8.5 l.7394-7396 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.5 |  |
| 189 | decision | Chose the ground tone over kerb hairlines as primary (a kerb line doubles linework at city zoom and fights the casing idiom; a value step separates street from block like FTG's paving tone and NG's street stripe). | — | §8.5 l.7396-7398 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 190 | rule | Way selection at the city band (VIS-3, N-6): tiers 1-2 always drawn; a tier-3 way is bounded when the footprint-covered share of its frontage is ≥ 0.8 on both sides and its width ≥ the mass closing's 1.5 px at the stop; a tier-3 way is drawn when access-critical (some plot has all its access edges on it and fronts no other drawn way) and not bounded; drawn tier-3 ways take the lane symbol (body = max(model width, 1 px), casing h/2); bounded intramural lanes and alleys stay unstroked gaps; suburb offshoots, ribbon back lanes, farm tracks and extramural lanes are therefore drawn. | ≥ 0.8 both sides; 1.5 px; body ≥ 1 px; casing h/2 | §8.5 l.7400-7408 | §8.2 symbolisation (lane symbol k=1, minPx 1) | rendering (§8) → §8.5 |  |

| 197 | rule | The neighbourhood band of every family (§8.2) is the stage-2 render tier (IMPROVEMENTS S5): hatched is NG's family; the stage-2 side-by-side against NG shows hatched with the ink close-up alongside, so a default-ink user opening a district sees the same palette with more detail (VIS-9). | — | §8.6 l.7428-7430 | II.4 render tier | rendering (§8) → §8.6 | II.4 (pipeline part) also describes the render tier. |
| 198 | rule | Close-up content (PART II) is derived on demand for visible tiles from the district's committed state and sub-seed — in the derivation pool, or in the generation worker when the pool is off (needs kernel and model) — and reaches the tile worker as DisplayDelta slices answering the tile's band request (§8.9), into the same mark tables and tile pyramid. | — | §8.6 l.7430-7433 | §8.9 band request; II.4 | rendering (§8) → §8.6 |  |
| 199 | rule | Stage 3 plugs in at the building band: marks from BuildingGenerator.compose(…, 'wings'\|'rooms') (takes a Kernel and a BuildingInput with a TerrainSampler, §2.6), computed on demand for visible buildings in the same place and cached by (entity key, revision, lod); the tile draws the outline until compositions arrive; every other band draws the outline, which equals the union of the composed wings by contract (SCL-13), so the footprint hash is the same at every band. | — | §8.6 l.7433-7438 | §2.6 BuildingGenerator | rendering (§8) → §8.6 |  |
| 200 | rule | The tile worker never runs compose() or close-up derivation: it holds no model and no terrain (N-4, §12.8). | — | §8.6 l.7438-7439 | §8.9; §12.8; §8.1 item 1 (backends never read the model) | rendering (§8) → §8.9 | conflict C01 |
| 201 | rule | Roof construction (§8.2) runs per stored wing (VIS-10) by BaseWing.kind (§2.6): rect → exact OBB ridges with hips from its four corners (BaseWing.corners when sides carry other vertices); a warp-de-rectangled rect is a quad by §2.6's carry rule; quad → exact OBB path if it passes the near-rectangle test, else the validated skeleton, gable constructions draw its stored ridge (arc-length midpoints of the two side chains ridgeAxis names; VP2-5); polygon → pyramid, cone or dome; the plain roof (no ridge or hip lines) is the downgrade (§3.8); two-tone lit/shadow faces per roof face by its normal against the light (faceLight). | — | §8.6 l.7439-7446 | §2.6 BaseWing; §3.8 | rendering (§8) → §8.6 | Wing-kind and ridge storage definitions are model §2.6's; roof drawing is rendering's. |
| 202 | rule | The derived-roof cache is keyed by (entity, revision, construction), so switching ink's hipped roofs to hatched's per-wing gables needs no regeneration. | — | §8.6 l.7446-7447 | §8.1 item 5 cache key | rendering (§8) → §8.1 item 5 |  |

### §8.8 Tiles, zoom stops, print, canvas budget (24 items)

| id | kind | statement | values | stated at | restated at | subject → home | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 207 | rule | Zoom stops (ZoomStop, declared in @plat/model, §14) at 2^¼ steps of scale (four per doubling) cover B-ZOOM's 1:50 000 → 1:500 in 27 stops; stop k has scale m_k metres per CSS pixel (N-9 a). | 2^¼; 27 stops; 1:50 000-1:500 | §8.8 l.7464-7466 | §8.1 l.6796 (ZoomStop in @plat/model) | rendering (§8) → §8.8 |  |
| 208 | rule | Tiles are 512 × 512 device px at the view's tile DPR d (device pixel ratio after the tier's cap) and world-aligned per (stop, d): tile (i, j) covers the world square of side 512 × m_k ÷ d whose corner is (i, j) × 512 × m_k ÷ d from the world origin — stable under panning and shared between views and exports at that stop and DPR. | 512 × 512 device px | §8.8 l.7466-7469 | §8.1 rationale (world-aligned tiles) | rendering (§8) → §8.8 |  |
| 209 | rule | A tile draws every mark whose bbox meets the tile dilated by margin m = the largest of casing half-width, drawn wall half-width, projected-shadow length, largest crown radius and halo width at the stop, in §8.1 item 3's slot-major order, identical in every tile; each tile clips to its own square, so no seams and no casing bar across a junction on a district boundary (GEO-28, N-7). | — | §8.8 l.7470-7474 | §8.1 item 3; §8.1 item 4 (dirty dilation) | rendering (§8) → §8.8 |  |
| 210 | rule | Fills of one semantic within a tile are one Path2D with the non-zero rule (a union: no AA seam between same-role neighbours); semantics declared seamClose (ground, masses, yard parts, shadow union) also get a ½-px same-colour stroke underneath (NG's seam dilation). | ½ px | §8.8 l.7475-7477 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.8 |  |
| 211 | test | Render tests: 0 casing pixels inside junction mouths on district boundaries; the census draw-order check (§8.3). | 0 pixels | §8.8 l.7477-7478 | §8.3 census rows | rendering (§8) → §8.3 |  |
| 212 | mechanism | Tile layers and composition (N-5): the tile worker rasters a base layer per tile (slots 1-20 of §8.2) and keeps it; it composes the base, the glyphs of labels placed at that stop (§9.2) and overlay live marks into one bitmap transferred to the main thread, one bitmap per tile position; a label edit, label toggle, selection or moving person re-composes touched tiles and never re-rasters a base. | re-compose ≈ 0.1 ms/tile + ≈ 1 µs/glyph | §8.8 l.7479-7483 | §8.2 slot split l.7171-7173; §12 budgets | rendering (§8) → §8.8 |  |
| 213 | rule | In path sun mode, on a tier whose budget admits it, the base is rastered as under (slots 1-9) and over (slots 11-20) with projected shadows (slot 10) in a layer of their own at half resolution (256² per tile; bilinear-scaled when composed), so a 1° sun step re-rasters only shadow layers and only a new light step (15°/5°) re-rasters under and over through §8.4's light pass. | under 1-9; shadow 10 at 256²; over 11-20 | §8.8 l.7483-7487 | §8.4 | rendering (§8) → §8.8 |  |
| 214 | decision | Chose composition in the tile worker over two tile layers committed separately to the main thread (halves the bitmaps committed at first paint and main-thread draws per frame at the same memory; a label change still never re-rasters the map). | — | §8.8 l.7487-7490 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 215 | rule | Tile key = (stop, i, j, tile DPR d, tile layer — base, or in path mode under/shadow/over —, style version, the view-state switches that change that layer's pixels: season, weather, shadows on/off and mode, model-layer toggles, base live layers; for the shadow layer (or, while projected shadows are on, base on a tier without shadow layers) the path-mode sun quantised to the tier's sun step (1°, or 3° after §8.4's fallback); for under and over (or base without shadow layers) of a sheet whose band programs read the light (faceLight, two-tone roof, hillshade, lit crowns) the light step; in fixed mode the sheet's sun belongs to the style version) (LC-23, N-8). | sun step 1° / 3° | §8.8 l.7491-7498 | §8.1 item 5 cache key; §8.4 | rendering (§8) → §8.8 | conflict C02 |
| 216 | rule | Content version = combination of the revisions of the marks drawn; a composed bitmap is keyed by its base key plus the label placement's revision at that stop and the overlay providers' versions. | — | §8.8 l.7498-7500 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.8 |  |
| 217 | rule | A DisplayDelta's dirty boxes invalidate every cached tile they meet, at every stop and DPR; in path mode a new 1° step invalidates only the layers keyed by it and a newer 1° step cancels an older one's pending re-rasters (latest wins); a new light step invalidates the layers keyed by the light step, which §8.4's light pass re-rasters to completion. | — | §8.8 l.7500-7503 | §8.4 l.7330-7342 | rendering (§8) → §8.8 |  |
| 218 | budget | Canvas budget per hardware tier (N-9 b): per visible tile position the worker's base (1 MB at 512²) and the main thread's composed bitmap (1 MB); next coarser stop's composed bitmaps kept for gestures (≤ 1 MB per visible position); fixed scratch: shadow and post-operation buffers (2 MB), crown library sprites (with a path sun in lit, one set per cached light step: the drawn step's set always, earlier sets while they fit, §8.10) and glyph atlases (≤ 5 MB). | 1 MB + 1 MB + ≤ 1 MB per position; 2 MB; ≤ 5 MB | §8.8 l.7504-7508 | §12.8; §8.10 crown cache | rendering (§8) → §8.8 | NR5-1: the export's crown set must be held until the export completes and counted in §12.8's export canvases. |
| 219 | budget | P = (⌈W·d ÷ 512⌉ + 1) × (⌈H·d ÷ 512⌉ + 1) worst-case tile positions for a W × H CSS-px view at tile DPR d; need = 3P + 7 MB, and 4.25P + 7 MB with path-mode shadow layers (under and over replace the base; the shadow layer adds ¼ MB). | 3P + 7 MB; 4.25P + 7 MB | §8.8 l.7508-7510 | §12.8 | rendering (§8) → §8.8 |  |

| 225 | rule | Gestures (SCL-7, VIS-6): during pinch or wheel zoom the compositor shows the nearest cached stop's composed tiles scaled (labels scale with them); 120 ms after input stops the view animates (≤ 150 ms) to the nearest stop and the tile worker re-rasters visible tiles centre-out, swapping each in when ready; panning composites cached tiles and shows newly exposed ones from the next coarser cached stop until rastered. | 120 ms; ≤ 150 ms | §8.8 l.7523-7527 | §12.5 | rendering (§8) → §8.8 |  |

| 228 | mechanism | Print and large PNG (O12 e): horizontal bands of tiles (512 device px high) are rastered and composed at the export scale; each band's scanlines are streamed into the PNG encoder, each row filtered by the per-row rule (of PNG's five filter types, the one whose filtered bytes have the smallest sum of absolute values, bytes read as signed; NR4-4), then one zlib stream (CompressionStream in browsers, zlib level 6 in Node), written as IDAT chunks band by band; §12.7 holds encoding time and fallback. | band 512 device px; zlib level 6 | §8.8 l.7532-7536 | §11.1 PNG; §12.7 | rendering (§8) → §8.8 |  |

| 231 | rule | Canvas2D is the reference backend of every shipped preset (ink, lit, hatched and variants), running on an OffscreenCanvas in the tile worker through resumable raster jobs (NR3-2); programs render typed-array paths, one Path2D per (program, semantic, variant) per tile, sprites by drawImage. | — | §8.9 l.7548-7550 | §8.2 referenceBackend; §8.2 lit on reference backend | rendering (§8) → §8.9 |  |
| 232 | rule | SVG is written from the same display list (§11.1). | — | §8.9 l.7551 | §11.1 | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 233 | rule | WebGL2 draws no marks: it exists only as a pre-decided fallback compositor that uploads composed Canvas2D tiles as textures, composites them with the view transform and runs post operations as one fragment pass — no stroke tessellation, no SDF text, no look that differs by backend (VIS-7). | — | §8.9 l.7552-7554 | §8.2 post (replaces WebGL2 hooks) | rendering (§8) → §8.9 |  |
| 234 | acceptance-bar | Measurement at M2b: pan and zoom frame rates of the Canvas2D compositor on reference and low-end devices at METROPOLIS scale (§12.5); the WebGL2 compositor is enabled for a hardware tier only if the Canvas2D compositor misses ≥ 60 fps (reference) or ≥ 30 fps (low end) there. | ≥ 60 fps ref; ≥ 30 fps low | §8.9 l.7554-7557 | §12.5 frame budgets | rendering (§8) → §8.9 |  |
| 235 | rule | Text (DEF-6): all text is drawn by Canvas2D in the tile worker's composition step (§8.8), glyph by glyph at positions from the bundled font's own metrics (§9.2), through a glyph atlas per (face, size, stop): each glyph drawn once into the atlas and copied per placement; SVG writes the same per-glyph positions — as the face's own glyph outlines by default, or as text in the embedded face on request (§11.1, N-13). | — | §8.9 l.7558-7561 | §9.2; §11.1 | rendering (§8) → §8.9 |  |
| 236 | decision | Chose Canvas2D glyphs composed onto tiles over SDF text (once tiles are composited text never needs the GPU; one text path keeps text identical on every output). | — | §8.9 l.7561-7563 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 237 | acceptance-bar | Measurement at M1: a tile's label composition ≤ 3 ms at ≤ 150 glyph runs; pre-decided fallback: whole words cached as bitmaps per (text, size, stop) in the atlas. | ≤ 3 ms at ≤ 150 glyph runs | §8.9 l.7563-7564 | §12 | rendering (§8) → §8.9 |  |
| 238 | rule | Threads (N-4) (1): the generation worker holds the document and model and runs S0-S14; also resolves instance overrides (§8.10), runs live-layer providers (§8.2), builds on-demand bands (district, neighbourhood, building bands of districts a band request names, via DisplayListBuilder.build(model, band, groups)), writes every export (§11.1: SVG from a transient list it builds and releases; raster formats from a transient list it transfers to the tile worker with the request; NR-5, NR3-6), answers the API's hit and inView queries (§11.2) from the model and its 24-B index (entity, bbox, group), and hands stage-3 compose() and close-up derivation (§8.6) to the derivation pool. | 24 B index | §8.9 l.7565-7572 | §8.1 item 6; §8.2 live layers; §8.6; §11.2 | rendering (§8) → §8.9 |  |
| 239 | rule | Threads (2): the derivation pool — up to three workers — runs the per-district stages at every rung (§12.3; merge protocol §4.1) and, on request, compose() and close-up derivation from the committed state of the district it is handed; with the pool off the generation worker runs them itself. | ≤ 3 workers | §8.9 l.7572-7575 | §12.3; §4.1; §8.6 | other: pipeline §4.1 → §12.3 (pool size) / §4.1 (merge protocol) | Pool size is §12.3's. |
| 240 | rule | Threads (3): the tile worker holds no model: it holds the display list with its label, instance and string tables (the only full copy, §8.1 item 6) and rasters base layers, runs S13b (§9.2) and decoration instancing (§8.10), composes labels and overlay live marks (§8.8), runs post operations, answers the main thread's furniture and snap queries and rasters raster exports from each request's transient list (§11.1; NR3-6), all through the queue. | — | §8.9 l.7575-7580 | §8.1 item 6; §8.6 l.7438; §12.8 | rendering (§8) → §8.9 | conflict C01 |
| 241 | rule | Threads (4): the main thread runs the UI, compositing of transferred tiles, pointer hit-testing on hit-test slices, screen-space furniture (§9.5; placement scores and ruler snaps asked of the tile worker) and input; nothing model-sized ever reaches the main thread. | — | §8.9 l.7580-7583 | §9.5 | rendering (§8) → §8.9 |  |

| 253 | algorithm-step | begin finds every mark whose bbox meets the dilated tile (§8.8) and fixes the draw sequence: native calls in §8.1 item 3's (slot, pass, z, key) order, each a fill or stroke of one batched Path2D, a run of ≤ 64 sprite draws (crowns, glyph copies), or a derived per-tile operation (projected-shadow union, raised under-copy, post operation). | ≤ 80 Path2D per tile (§12.5); ≤ 64 sprites per run | §8.9 l.7619-7622 | §12.5 (≤ 80 Path2D) | rendering (§8) → §8.9 |  |
| 254 | rule | The JavaScript that prepares a call reads the clock after every mark and every 64 instances and can stop there; a native call is never split (a split batched fill would draw AA seams); a derived operation (using shared shadow and post buffers) runs whole inside one step; overrun past the deadline is at most one native call or one derived operation. | every 64 instances | §8.9 l.7623-7627 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.9 |  |
| 255 | rule | Each job owns its canvas and context state; deltas are applied between steps; a removed mark becomes a dead row and an added mark a new row; the tile worker compacts its list only while no job is live; a job whose tile a delta's dirty boxes meet is cancelled and queued again. Slicing changes when calls run, never which or in what order: a tile rastered whole and in 1-ms slices is pixel-identical (§8.3 census row). | 1-ms slices | §8.9 l.7628-7632 | §8.3 sliced rasters row | rendering (§8) → §8.9 |  |
| 256 | number | The queue calls step(8) — step(4) after the pre-decided fallback. | 8 ms; 4 ms fallback | §8.9 l.7632-7633 | §12.5 | rendering (§8) → §8.9 |  |
| 257 | mechanism | Tile worker's queue (NR-4, NR3-6): between two steps it serves its message queue, then runs one step of the highest class with ready work: class 1 furniture and snap queries (answer due within one frame); class 2 incoming deltas (edit deltas, band answers, live-layer deliveries) in arrival order, each O(changed marks), ≤ 1 MB per answer; class 3 the visible view: S13b at the view's stop (resumable, one unit at a time, §9.2; NR4-2), with a path sun after a new light step the lit crown bake (one sprite per unit) and §8.4's light pass, visible tiles' raster jobs centre-out, compositions and commits, re-compositions for label and overlay changes; class 4 exports: first S13b at the export's stop over its transient list (memo held by the job), then band by band the raster jobs, composition, read-back (64 rows per step) and encoding. | 4 classes; 64 rows per read-back step | §8.9 l.7635-7643 | §9.2; §8.4; §11.1 | rendering (§8) → §8.9 |  |
| 258 | rule | A class runs only while every higher class is empty, one step at a time; within a class the job in progress runs to completion before the next begins (at most one raster job per class live); a query waits at most one step; a pan or zoom during an export pre-empts it within one step. | — | §8.9 l.7645-7648 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.9 |  |
| 259 | rule | No starvation: an export that has waited 100 ms since its last step runs its next step ahead of class 3 (never ahead of classes 1 and 2) — at most one step in any 100 ms, ≤ 8 % of the worker — so an animated view slows an export but cannot stop it. | 100 ms; ≤ 8 % | §8.9 l.7648-7650 | §12.7 export beside view | rendering (§8) → §8.9 |  |
| 260 | budget | A settle contains at most two export steps at CITY (≤ 150 ms) and three at METROPOLIS (≤ 250 ms): ≤ 16 and ≤ 24 ms beside the ≈ 42-83 ms settle rasters of §12.5. | 2 / 3 steps; ≤ 16 / ≤ 24 ms; ≈ 42-83 ms | §8.9 l.7650-7652 | §12.5 | rendering (§8) → §8.9 | Settle figures (150/250 ms, 42-83 ms) are §12.5's. |
| 261 | rule | A newer sun step or a tile leaving the view cancels its job (latest wins; §8.4, §8.8); the export dialog shows the tile worker's progress messages (bands done of total) and, while class 3 holds the worker, that the export is waiting for the view. | — | §8.9 l.7652-7654 | §8.4; §8.8 | rendering (§8) → §8.9 |  |
| 262 | mechanism | Placement is stepped like a raster (NR4-2): S13b (view's in class 3, export's in class 4) is a PlacementJob (§9.2) called with step(8) (step(4) after the fallback); unit = one decision (a label's rung-(5) test, one pin of the pin pass, one push or one local-evaluation decision, or glyph layout of one placed label); reads the clock after every unit, so overrun ≤ one unit (≈ 1 µs on average, tens of µs for the longest name). | step(8)/step(4); ≈ 1 µs per unit | §8.9 l.7656-7660 | §9.2 PlacementJob | rendering (§9) → §9.2 |  |

| 264 | rule | Queries to the tile worker (NR-4): compass, title and legend placement (§9.5) and the ruler's snapping read the display list, which only the tile worker holds; the main thread asks through the query rows and the tile worker answers with ObstacleSource.coverage and RoleIndex.snap over its list — the implementations S13b uses (§14). | — | §8.9 l.7666-7669 | §9.5; §14 | rendering (§8) → §8.9 |  |

'deciduous'\|'evergreen' } — what a restyle or add override sets (§7.1). | scale 0.5-1.5; tone −1…1 | §8.10 l.7709-7710 | §7.1 override ops | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 276 | rule | One instance set per entity, independent of the band: every instances mark (wood, garden, park, copse, orchard, avenue, hedgerow, yard planting, scatter class) has one set of positions in one of three layouts: stamp of a per-class blue-noise tile for areas; jittered grid for orchards (6-8 m); jittered line for avenues and street trees (along the row §4.9 derives in the planting district's own half-corridor strip at Greenery.along's offsetCm from the kerb, no plat edge) and for hedgerows along their L3 field-boundary chains (8-12 m along the line). | grid 6-8 m; hedgerow 8-12 m | §8.10 l.7713-7718 | §4.9 | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 277 | rule | S14 draws each layout's parameters — offset, rotation, spacing in whole cm inside its class's range (§4.9; 300-500 cm woods, 400-600 cm gardens) — from the entity's render.instances stream (§6.1) in the generation worker and carries them in the mark's InstanceTable row (§8.1). | woods 300-500 cm; gardens 400-600 cm | §8.10 l.7718-7720 | §4.9 (spacing ranges); §8.1 item 2 | other: pipeline §4.9 → §4.9 (spacing ranges); §8.10 (draw) | Spacing ranges are §4.9's; §8.10 restates them. |
| 278 | number | Stamp tiles (NR3-7, NR4-1): per stamped class four toroidal blue-noise tiles of 6 400 × 6 400 cm at reference spacing r₀ = 400 cm (≈ 162 points each, fixall_rendering_r5_seamstrip.out), each point carrying a progressive rank (void-and-cluster order: every prefix is evenly spread; the lowest-rank quarter keeps ≈ 1.5-1.8 × r₀ between any two points). | 6 400 cm; r₀ = 400 cm; ≈ 162 points; 1.5-1.8 × r₀ | §8.10 l.7721-7724 | §8.10 construction; §8.3 stamp rows | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 279 | defined-term | Seam-compatible tiles: the four tiles share one border band — every point with edge distance e = min(x, 6 400 − x, y, 6 400 − y) below r₀ ÷ 2 — and differ only inside it. | e < r₀/2 = 200 cm | §8.10 l.7724-7726 | §8.10 construction (e < 200 cm) | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 280 | rule | An entity whose spacing is r stamps the tiles scaled by s = r ÷ r₀, so one tile set serves every spacing of the class (a wood at 3 m stamps 48-m tiles, a garden at 6 m 96-m tiles). | s = r / r₀ | §8.10 l.7726-7728 | §8.10 Stamp | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 281 | invariant | No two instances of one stamp lie closer than the entity's spacing (less ≤ 1.5 cm of centimetre rounding): inside a cell because each tile is spaced at r₀ toroidally; across every seam because outside-band points are ≥ r₀/2 from cell edges and a band point is present at the same position in the other point's tile and was spaced against it there; every band draws a subset (ranks below s_b), so it holds at every band. | ≤ 1.5 cm rounding | §8.10 l.7728-7734 | §8.10 Stamp (1.5 cm bound); §8.3 seam test | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 282 | evidence | Measured on five seed sets (fixall_rendering_r5_seamstrip.out): 0 pairs closer than r₀ across cells in all 256 arrangements of 2 × 2 cells, for all ranks and for rank < 0.6 and < 0.25 subsets; four independent tiles have 3.5-5 such pairs per 64-m seam (NR4-1). | 0 pairs; 256 arrangements; 3.5-5 pairs | §8.10 l.7734-7737 | §8.3 seam test | rendering (§8) → §8.10 | Keep as a pointer. |
| 283 | rule | The tile variant used by a stamp cell is RH32('stamp.variant', key, salt, u, v) mod 4 (§8.1 item 5), breaking visible repetition: only the band, ≈ 11-14 % of a tile's points, repeats in every cell. | mod 4; ≈ 11-14 % | §8.10 l.7737-7738 | §8.10 l.7772 (≈ 12 %) | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 284 | rule | The tiles are built once, when the product is built, and shipped; nothing samples or ranks at run time. | — | §8.10 l.7738-7739 | §8.10 shipped asset | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 285 | rule | One construction everywhere (NR-6, NR3-7, NR4-1): a class's band depends on the class alone, each tile on (class, tile index) and the band; all built by plat/tools/stamptiles/ (build-time script in the product repository, like the texture set's) with @plat/kernel's poisson module (§14), held to §6.5's allowlist lint like every model package, so any machine builds the same bytes. | — | §8.10 l.7740-7743 | §14; §6.5 | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 286 | rule | Stamp-tile random source: rng.stream (§6.1, §14) with a constant entity key — §6.1's four-lane finaliser over (fixed stamp-tile domain constant, decoration class's registry index, reference spacing 400 cm, creation index 0-3 for the tiles and 4 for the band) —, stage STAGE_VIEW, the non-rerollable concern render.stampTiles (§6.1 registry), reroll, attempt, epoch and salt 0. | creation index 0-3 tiles, 4 band | §8.10 l.7743-7747 | §6.1 concern registry | other: determinism §6.1 → §6.1 (stream key rule); §8.10 (key words) | Stream-key composition is determinism's; key words may stay here. |
| 287 | algorithm-step | Sampling: Bridson with k = 50 candidates, all evaluated; every accepted point rounded at once to integer cm inside the 6 400-cm tile so the spacing test is an exact integer comparison of squared toroidal distances with r₀²; first point (int(0, 6 399), int(0, 6 399)); active point int(0, n − 1) over the active list in insertion order, a retired point replaced by the list's last; each candidate at radius r₀ × (1 + float01) along angle 2π × float01 (cos/sin from dmath, §6.5); candidates tried in draw order. | k = 50 | §8.10 l.7747-7753 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 288 | algorithm-step | The band (creation index 4): a full toroidal tile sampled as above and cut to its points with e < 200 cm, in index order. Each tile (creation index 0-3): the band's points pre-placed in that order (spacing grid and initial active list), sampling continues over e ≥ 200 cm (no first point drawn; a candidate with e < 200 cm rejected); the tile is the band's points followed by its own in acceptance order. | e < 200 cm | §8.10 l.7753-7757 | §8.10 l.7725-7726 (r₀/2) | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 289 | algorithm-step | Ranking per tile: every point's energy = sum over other points in point-index order of exp(−d² ÷ (2σ²)), d toroidal distance in cm, σ = r₀, exponential dmath.exp; the highest-energy point (tie: lower index) is removed and takes the highest free rank (n − 1 first); every remaining point has the removed point's term subtracted in point-index order (removal update), costing ≈ 1.5 n² exponentials (O(n²)), never a re-sum (O(n³): ≈ 53 ms vs ≈ 1.8 ms per tile at 161 points); a needed square root is Math.sqrt, exact by specification (§6.5 (2)). | σ = r₀; ≈ 1.5 n²; ≈ 53 ms vs ≈ 1.8 ms | §8.10 l.7757-7763 | §6.5 (2) | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 290 | rule | The crown library's shapes use dmath the same way, so only their rasterisation into sprites is engine-specific. | — | §8.10 l.7763-7764 | §8.10 crowns | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 291 | number | A class's band and four tiles build in ≈ 19.5 ms (measured cost model, fixall_rendering_r5_seamstrip.out). | ≈ 19.5 ms | §8.10 l.7764-7765 | §8.10 fallback l.7782-7783 | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 292 | decision | Chose to cut the band from a full tile over Bridson restricted to the band (band sampled alone packs points against its inner edge: 4.7-5.3 per 100 m² vs 2.8-3.6 inside, a density stripe; a cut keeps 3.6-4.4 everywhere; price: largest empty circle 1.20 × r₀ vs ≤ 1.08 × r₀, 0.11 % vs 0.04 % area could take another point). | see statement | §8.10 l.7765-7771 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 293 | decision | Chose a band of r₀ ÷ 2 over r₀: both give 0 close pairs across seams, while the narrower band repeats ≈ 12 % of a tile's points in every cell instead of ≈ 23 % (NR4-1). | ≈ 12 % vs ≈ 23 % | §8.10 l.7771-7773 | §8.10 l.7738 (≈ 11-14 %) | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 294 | interface | Shipped stamp-tile asset (NR3-7): plat/assets/stamptiles/stamptiles.bin holds, per stamped class in registry order, its four tiles — each self-contained, band points first: a point count, then per point x and y as int16 cm and rank as u16 — with a header naming asset version, format version, generating script and its constant key words (§17.1 (5)) and the stored H128 of every tile. | 6 B/point; ≈ 1 KB/tile; ≈ 31 KB for 8 classes | §8.10 l.7774-7778 | §17.1 (5) | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 295 | rule | @plat/render bundles the asset bytes into the code of both workers, so no host serves an extra file (§11.2); at worker start each worker takes typed-array views and checks every tile's H128 against the stored value. | ≈ 0.03-0.06 ms for 8-16 classes | §8.10 l.7778-7781 | §8.3 stamp-tile census; §11.2 | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 296 | acceptance-bar | Pre-decided fallback when a tile's hash differs (damaged bundle): that worker rebuilds the class's band and tiles with the same poisson construction at start-up, before its first district-band raster (≈ 19.5 ms per class at r₀), reported; a rebuilt tile that still differs is a determinism defect, which the census's rebuild check (§8.3) fails in CI. | ≈ 19.5 ms per class | §8.10 l.7781-7784 | §8.3 | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 297 | rule | Release changes: a new stamped class adds its four tiles and moves no stored hash (no drawn wood moves); a construction change (r₀, σ, k, tile side, band width, key words) moves stored hashes and is recorded as a render.stampTiles view shift (§13.3), bumping the style version of every shipped sheet that draws stamped classes; a new spacing needs nothing (a scale). | — | §8.10 l.7784-7787 | §13.3 view shift | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 298 | history | The shared band (NR4-1) changed the construction before any asset or golden existed (goldens start at M1, §13.3), so it moves no recorded hash and needs no shift record. | — | §8.10 l.7788-7789 | — | history (validation record; drop from normative text) → validation record |  |
| 299 | decision | Chose a constant key over the seed or document for stamp tiles (a library shared by every document, session and host; per-town variety comes from the stamp's offset, rotation, spacing and seed-dependent tile variant; a seed-keyed library would be rebuilt per document for no visible gain). | — | §8.10 l.7789-7793 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 300 | algorithm-step | Stamp (NR4-3): from the row, offset o ∈ [0, 6 400)² in whole reference cm, rotationCdeg ∈ [0, 36 000) (S14 draws int(0, 35 999)), scale s = spacing ÷ 400 cm; R_θ's cos and sin = dmath.cos/dmath.sin of rotationCdeg × (π ÷ 18 000) (a double literal); tile point t of cell (u, v) lies at R_θ · (s · (6 400 · (u, v) + t + o)), each coordinate rounded to whole cm (Math.round) = the instance's world position, the one position the face test, density test, drawing, override resolution and every export read. | — | §8.10 l.7794-7799 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 301 | algorithm-step | Stamp cells are those of the rotated, scaled lattice covering the entity's face (bbox taken into lattice coordinates by the same cos/sin, widened by one cell); a point survives if its world position is inside the face (exact point-in-polygon with holes on integer cm, §3.2) and, for woods, passes the edge test of a domain-warped density evaluated once per point at its world position in metres. | widen by 1 cell | §8.10 l.7799-7803 | §3.2 | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 302 | invariant | Rounding moves a point by ≤ 0.71 cm, so two instances keep the entity's spacing less ≤ 1.5 cm. | ≤ 0.71 cm; ≤ 1.5 cm | §8.10 l.7803-7804 | §8.10 l.7729 | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 303 | defined-term | Stamp ordinal = (u, v, p): stable — editing the face's boundary changes only which edge points survive, never the interior's ordinals or positions. | — | §8.10 l.7804-7805 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 304 | rule | One result on every engine: the stamp, grid and line layouts, the per-instance look draws and the density test, with everything they call, are held to §6.5 (3)'s allowlist lint, so they compute the same integer positions and draws on every engine, and a GeoJSON, glTF or CityJSON file with trees is byte-identical on every host (§11.1; the five-engine golden run compares one, §13.3). | — | §8.10 l.7805-7809 | §11.1 byte-identity; §13.3; §6.5 (3) | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 305 | decision | Chose integer-cm world positions computed with dmath over doubles from Math.cos (model-format exporters promise byte-identical files; a last-bit cosine difference can move a coordinate across GeoJSON's 7-decimal ≈ 1 cm rounding; the cm is the model's lattice, §3.2, so the face test is exact) (NR4-3). | 7 decimals ≈ 1 cm | §8.10 l.7809-7812 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 306 | algorithm-step | Grid: one point per cell (u, v) of the rotated lattice at the row's spacing, jittered ± 15 % of spacing in x and y from two RH32 draws over (key, salt, u, v) (tags grid.jitter.x, grid.jitter.y), rounded to whole cm and kept if inside the face; ordinal = (u, v). | ± 15 % | §8.10 l.7813-7815 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 307 | algorithm-step | Line: point i at arc length (i + ½) × spacing from the line's start end (the end whose junction address sorts first; for an along row, the end at its way's first junction), jittered along and across the same way and rounded to whole cm; ordinal = i. Park paths are L3 path faces drawn by the path program, not instances. | (i + ½) × spacing | §8.10 l.7815-7818 | — | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 308 | rule | Rank and band thinning (N-10): every instance has a normalised rank r ∈ [0, 1): stamp = progressive rank ÷ tile point count; grid = 32-bit bit-reversal of the Morton interleave of (u, v) ÷ 2³² (2-D van der Corput); line = bit-reversal of i ÷ 2³². Band b draws instances with r < s_b (city: none — the wood, orchard or green fill; district 0.6; neighbourhood and finer 1), so every coarser band is a subset of the finer: an instance keeps its position once visible and appears at most once in a zoom sweep (LC-20, VIS-5); census covers all three layouts (§8.3). | s_b: city none, district 0.6, neighbourhood+ 1 | §8.10 l.7819-7825 | §8.3 decoration stability row; §8.10 l.7733-7734 | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 309 | rule | Per-instance look keyed by (key, ordinal): species drawn by its share from Greenery.species (inner or outer share by distance from the edge) picks the crown class; crown index into the style's crown library; colour jitter; scale jitter ± 15 %; leafType (from the species when present, deciduous otherwise) — all RH32 draws over (render key, ordinal, concern salt); hatch phase and roof detail keyed by the entity's key; nothing keyed by band or interned handle. | scale jitter ± 15 % | §8.10 l.7826-7837 | §8.1 item 5 | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 310 | rule | Crown library: 20 crowns per class per style, built once per session from rng.stream keyed by the sheet's key (hash of id@version), STAGE_VIEW, the concern style.crowns with its salt, and class and index i as creation index (§6.1's structured components; the path form is a report rendering, §14), drawn as sprites. | 20 per class per style | §8.10 l.7827-7831 | §8.3 catalogue (20 per class) | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 311 | rule | A lit sheet bakes its crown sprites per (style, light step): once with a fixed sun, and with a path sun again at each light step, in the tile worker as one-sprite units of §8.9's queue; the drawn step's set is always kept and earlier sets while they fit §8.8's crown-and-atlas scratch (§8.4; NR4-5). | — | §8.10 l.7831-7834 | §8.2 l.7218-7219; §8.4 light pass; §8.8 canvas budget; §8.9 class 3 | rendering (§8) → §8.10 | OPEN NR5-1: an export in lit with a path sun must bake its crown set as class-4 one-sprite units before its first band when not cached and hold it until the export completes.; conflict C02 |
| 312 | interface | Instance edit event { t: 'instance', entity, ordinal \| point, op 'add'\|'move'\|'remove'\|'restyle', to?, style? } (§7.1), applied after the set is generated: remove drops the ordinal; move replaces its position; add appends a user ordinal u<n> at point; restyle sets its InstanceStyle; for an ordinal-addressed event the dry run records the instance's generated position in point (LC-20, NF-D11). | — | §8.10 l.7838-7841 | §7.1 event list | other: determinism §7.1 → §7.1 (event shape); §8.10 (semantics) | Event record belongs to §7.1's event union. |
| 313 | rule | Override resolution runs in the generation worker whenever a commit touches an entity carrying overrides (re-runs this function for that entity only): applies by ordinal when the entity's lineage is unchanged since the event (no carry lifecycle event and no supersession after the event's seq, §6.3) and that ordinal's generated position lies within 0.5 × spacing of the recorded point; otherwise (carried successor, superseded entity — overrides move to the supersededBy successor —, or a moved ordinal) re-matches by position to the nearest unclaimed generated instance within 0.5 × spacing (candidates in (distance, ordinal) order, overrides in log order); else kept orphaned and reported. | 0.5 × spacing | §8.10 l.7841-7849 | §6.3 | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 314 | rule | Resolution reads each positional override's current position from its entity's overrides table (§2.7) — a move's to, an add's point, and for a line layout the recorded generated point — which the event writes when it applies and a warp or vertex move displaces with the host face (§7.4; VK2-13); for stamp and grid layouts the recorded generated point stays world-anchored. | — | §8.10 l.7849-7852 | §2.7; §7.4 | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 315 | rule | An add override stays while its point lies in the entity's face (or within 0.5 × spacing of its line) and is orphaned otherwise; resolved overrides (ordinal, op, position, style, state byOrdinal/rematched/orphaned) cross to the tile worker as the InstanceTable's override rows (§8.1, §8.9); a tree brush is a batch of instance events (COV-FTG-M04). | 0.5 × spacing | §8.10 l.7852-7855 | §8.1 InstanceTable; §8.9 crossings | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 316 | decision | Chose to apply by ordinal only while lineage and position both hold over whenever the address survives (a §6.3 carried successor keeps the address but not the lineage, so its lineage-keyed stamp puts the same ordinal on a different tree). | — | §8.10 l.7855-7857 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 317 | rule | Where it runs (N-4): instances generated per tile in the tile worker from the InstanceTable row and memoised per (key, revision, style version) for the session; never written to the document except as overrides; the generation worker runs the same function on the same shipped tiles (NR-6, NR3-7) for entities with overrides (to resolve) and for exports: GeoJSON, glTF and CityJSON exporters call it at the finest band with overrides applied and write each instance's position, species, crown diameter and leaf type (§11.1; VN-158, XB32); the bench computes tree statistics from it (SCL-21). | — | §8.10 l.7858-7863 | §11.1 tree export; §8.9 threads | rendering (§8) → §8.10 |  |
| 318 | budget | Placement ≤ 1 µs per instance (stamp, scale, rounding and point-in-polygon), drawing ≤ 2 µs per instance as a sprite (§12.7); the stamp-tile asset ≤ 5 ms per worker per session, loaded and checked at worker start, never on a settle (§12.7); the crown library's build, and in lit with a path sun each light step's bake, ≤ 20 ms of tile-worker time in one-sprite units, built when the style loads, before any district-band raster needs it (§12.7; NR4-5). | ≤ 1 µs; ≤ 2 µs; ≤ 5 ms; ≤ 20 ms | §8.10 l.7864-7868 | §12.7; §8.4 l.7346 | rendering (§12) → §12.7 | Budgets restated from §12.7. |
| 319 | decision | Chose the stamped ranked tile over per-entity Bridson (≈ 12 µs per tree; a 36 ha wood takes 170 ms, redteam-scale §7.2; a stamp < 1 µs; ranked tiles give subsets by construction). | ≈ 12 µs/tree; 170 ms | §8.10 l.7870-7872 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 320 | decision | Chose bit-reversal ranks for jittered layouts over a second blue-noise ranking (van der Corput is a pure function of the ordinal) (N-10). | — | §8.10 l.7872-7874 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 321 | decision | Chose to build tiles at build time and ship them over a per-session build (the session build ran on the interaction path under §12.5's ≤ 150 ms settle with no budget, in both workers; a build-time asset costs a bundled ≈ 31 KB and a hash check) (NR3-7). | ≈ 31 KB; ≤ 150 ms | §8.10 l.7874-7877 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md | Contains history ('the session build ran'). |
| 322 | decision | Chose one tile set per class scaled by spacing over one per (class, spacing) (per-spacing library has hundreds of members per class, ≈ 19 ms each, megabytes as an asset; uniform scaling keeps blue noise and ordinals) (NR3-7). | ≈ 19 ms each | §8.10 l.7877-7880 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 323 | decision | Chose four seam-compatible variants sharing a border band over one larger toroidal tile per class (MFCG's/VG's single tile, here 128 m at r₀, repeats positions on a periodic lattice; variants keep ≈ 88 % of each cell's points varying at the same asset size) and over independent variants (3.5-5 close pairs per 64-m seam) (NR4-1). | 128 m; ≈ 88 % | §8.10 l.7880-7885 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |

### §9 introduction (1 items)

| id | kind | statement | values | stated at | restated at | subject → home | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 324 | summary | Plain-words intro restates §9.1-§9.5: district names curve along the district middle, never cross an inner river nor cover gate, bridge or keep; too-small districts labelled as named quarters; street names appear on zoom; labels never collide; identical placement everywhere via shipped font metrics; names are model data, placement is view data; scale bar, compass, grid, title, legend, emblem and ruler are drawn on top and each is movable, restylable and switchable. | — | §9 intro l.7891-7897 | — | rendering (§9) → §9.1-§9.5 | Pure restatement. |

### §9.1 Model and view (12 items)

| id | kind | statement | values | stated at | restated at | subject → home | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 325 | rule | Model (S13a; in the model hash; persisted): every entity's name and short form, a LabelEntity per labelled thing with its candidates, and the quarter merge tree (§9.4). | — | §9.1 l.7903-7904 | §4 S13a | rendering (§9) → §9.1 |  |
| 326 | interface | LabelEntity { id; target; kind 'district'\|'quarter'\|'way'\|'landmark'\|'water'\|'wood'\|'gate'\|'title'\|'annotation'\|'address'; text: NameRef; shortText?: NameRef; priority; lod: LodBand[]; candidates { spines: Polyline[] (area and path labels, world cm, preference order); anchors { p; dirCdeg? }[] (point labels); breakAt? (allowed two-line breaks) }; pinned?: PinnedPlacement (user placement, model data); hidden? }. | 10 kinds | §9.1 l.7907-7911 | §8.1 LabelTable (view copy) | rendering (§9) → §9.1 | A model record, declared only here (grep); §14 copies LabelPlacer's use of it.; conflict C05, C06 |
| 327 | interface | QuarterNode { id; members (districts); children [Id, Id] \| null; name: NameRef; spine: Polyline; cityCut (one of the min(N_q, L) quarters of the city cut, §4.5; L = number of districts: the city band's area labels) }; the tree is built by S6 (§4.5), named by S13a; every node has its own LabelEntity (kind 'quarter'). | min(N_q, L) | §9.1 l.7912-7913 | §4.5; §9.4 quarter tree | other: pipeline §4.5 → §4.5 (tree built) / §9.1 (type) |  |
| 328 | interface | Placement { stop (zoom stop where the user placed it); sizePx (cap height in display px at 1×, never below the kind's floor, §9.4); anchor: PointCm (point labels: text origin; area/path: spine midpoint); spine?: PolylineCm; dirCdeg?; lines? 1\|2 (two lines broken at the entity's first breakAt) } — a user placement set by moveLabel (§7.1), model data (N-28). | — | §9.1 l.7914-7919 | §7.1 moveLabel | rendering (§9) → §9.1 |  |
| 329 | interface | PinnedPlacement extends Placement { seq } — the seq of the moveLabel event that set it (§7.1); applying the event writes pinned = { ...placement, seq }; a warp or vertex move carrying the pin keeps it; it orders §9.2's pin pass (NR3-3). | — | §9.1 l.7920-7921 | §8.1 LabelTable.pinSeq | rendering (§9) → §9.1 |  |
| 330 | rule | View (S13b; never hashed; recomputed per zoom stop and cached, §9.2): which candidate each label uses, its size and glyph positions, the quarter cut, the halo roles. | — | §9.1 l.7924-7925 | §9.2 | rendering (§9) → §9.1 |  |

| 342 | rule | Between stops the view uses the placement of the stop at or coarser than the current scale: zooming in only shrinks every label's world box, so no collision appears; crossing below a stop switches to that coarser stop's placement; zero collisions hold at every zoom, not only at stops. | — | §9.2 l.7964-7966 | §9.2 pins l.8028 | rendering (§9) → §9.2 |  |
| 343 | rule | The candidate set of a stop is fixed before the greedy: every LabelTable row drawn at the band, minus labels §9.4's rung (5) hands to a quarter at this stop, plus one candidate per QuarterNode rung (5) activates (created once however many members yield, not again when the stop's cut already labels that node) with the highest priority among its yielding members, the node's area and the node's own label id (N-11); rung (5) is decided from geometry alone (whether a label fits its own face at the floor). | — | §9.2 l.7967-7972 | §9.4 rung (5) | rendering (§9) → §9.2 |  |

| 345 | rule | The whole-map greedy is evaluated locally: deciding c first decides (memoised) every higher candidate whose alternate boxes meet c's; the dependency graph is acyclic because the order is strict, so the result is independent of tile order and viewport. | — | §9.2 l.7976-7978 | — | rendering (§9) → §9.2 |  |

| 348 | algorithm-step | A placement unit is one of: the rung-(5) test of one LabelTable row; one pin of the pin pass; one step of the local evaluation on an explicit stack (push the highest undecided candidate whose alternate boxes meet the top's, or when all such are decided decide the top, write the memo, pop); or the glyph layout of one placed label. The job takes the scope's candidates as roots in strict order, pins first. | — | §9.2 l.7996-8001 | §8.9 l.7657-7660 (unit list restated) | rendering (§9) → §9.2 | conflict C01 |
| 349 | rule | The stack and memo persist between steps; every decision reads only already-decided candidates, so step placement never changes the result: a stop placed whole and one unit per step give identical placement digests (§8.3, §13.3); a unit costs ≈ 1 µs on average and tens of µs at most, so a step overruns by one unit. | ≈ 1 µs avg | §9.2 l.8001-8005 | §8.9 l.7659-7660; §8.3 sliced placement row | rendering (§9) → §9.2 |  |
| 350 | rule | The view's job reads the tile worker's list, which deltas change between steps: a delta applied between two steps drops the memo entries its dirty boxes invalidate and, when it changes a label row or an obstacle of the job's stop, the job is begun again keeping every memo entry left valid; an export's job reads its own transient list, which nothing changes (§11.1). | — | §9.2 l.8005-8009 | §11.1 transient list | rendering (§9) → §9.2 | conflict C01 |
| 351 | rule | Pinned labels (N-28, NR3-3): a pinned label is shown only in its bands; at its own stop and every finer stop it is placed exactly as the user left it, before any other candidate, by the pin pass: pins eligible at the stop (in their bands, own stop at or coarser than this stop) taken in moveLabel seq order then label id (LabelTable.pinSeq, §8.1); each whose box meets no already-fixed pin becomes a fixed pin (an obstacle every candidate avoids); one whose box meets a fixed pin is hidden at that stop and reported ('pinned label hidden: overlaps pin P at stop k'). | — | §9.2 l.8010-8016 | §9.1 l.7925-7927 | rendering (§9) → §9.2 |  |
| 352 | rule | The pin pass is a greedy in strict order evaluated locally (deciding a pin decides, memoised, every earlier pin whose box meets it), so it is tile-order independent; a pin's box is sized in pixels, so a pin fixed at stop k meets nothing new at finer stops and a pin hidden at k is fixed again where the boxes part. | — | §9.2 l.8016-8020 | — | rendering (§9) → §9.2 |  |
| 353 | rule | moveLabel's dry run (§7.1) refuses a placement whose box at its stop meets another pinned label's box there, returning the nearest free position as the conflict's action (interactive guard); the pin pass is the guarantee because pins also come together without moveLabel: a warp or vertex move (§9.1, §7.4), a setAttr rename or ids-scoped rerollLayer(naming) lengthening a pin's text (§9.6), a setStyle changing face widths. | — | §9.2 l.8020-8024 | §7.1 | rendering (§9) → §9.2 |  |
| 354 | rule | At a coarser stop a pin's world box grows, so it is not fixed: it keeps its anchor and spine and enters the greedy at its entity's priority with that placement as its only alternate — drawn when it meets no higher candidate, obstacle or fixed pin, otherwise hidden at that stop and reported ('pinned label hidden at stops coarser than k'); zero collisions hold at every zoom with pins included. | — | §9.2 l.8025-8028 | §9.1 l.7926-7927 | rendering (§9) → §9.2 |  |

| 356 | rule | Scope: area and way labels for all candidates of the band; addresses and repeated street names only for candidates in visible tiles and their dependency closure; memo keyed by (stop, candidate id, candidate revision). | — | §9.2 l.8032-8033 | §9.2 PlacementJob.cancel comment | rendering (§9) → §9.2 |  |
| 357 | rule | Incremental (SCL-9): after an edit, memo entries of candidates whose boxes meet the dirty boxes are dropped and re-decided; change propagates only through candidates whose outcome changes. | — | §9.2 l.8034-8036 | §9.2 l.8006-8008 | rendering (§9) → §9.2 |  |

| 364 | algorithm-step | Area labels (districts, quarters, water bodies, woods; GEO-39): simplify the face with the kernel's topology-preserving Visvalingam (simplify, §3.4; a removal is refused when the new edge would cross another edge of the face or its holes) to half the label's cap height at the band's coarsest stop; hole-aware straight skeleton (skeleton(rings, 'spine'), §3.8, gated by its validator: connected, one independent cycle per hole); each skeleton edge's clearance = smaller event time of its ends; maximum-clearance spanning tree (Kruskal by clearance descending, ties by edge index) drops each cycle's lowest-clearance edge; spine = longest path of that tree by length (two linear passes), smoothed (Visvalingam → centripetal Catmull-Rom). | ½ cap height | §9.3 l.8062-8070 | §3.4; §3.8 | rendering (§9) → §9.3 |  |
