### §9.6 Names (5 items)

| id | kind | statement | values | stated at | restated at | subject → home | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 396 | rule | Names come from the original grammar of §10.4, conditioned on the model (docks district for its water, gate ward for its gate, street for its destination or trade — R16), drawn from the entity's naming stream keyed from its entity key (§6.1), only for an entity with no predecessor, under a town-wide rerollLayer(naming), under an ids-scoped one naming it, or for an unedited name whose naming inputs changed (a grammar row it used, or the corpus a field of the culture or ancestry row names — a (row, field) slice, §6.3 case (4), §7.6; VD4-5); a successor takes its predecessor's name and naming record with its address (VD2-10, VD3-2). | — | §9.6 l.8184-8189 | §10.4; §6.3 case (4) | other: pipeline §10.4 / determinism §6.3 → §10.4 (grammar) / §6.3 (when drawn) | Naming is owned by §10.4 and §6.3; §9.6 largely restates them. |
| 397 | rule | Uniqueness (LC-17) within kind — districts, quarters and water bodies per town; ways per district; tier-1 ways town-wide: in a full generation names resolved in id order and a later entity repeating an earlier name re-draws on its own …/attempt=n (≤ 8), then takes a distinguishing suffix; in a scoped regeneration or edit, entities outside the scope are fixed and only those inside re-draw; an existing entity is never renamed by another's creation; the locality oracle resolves collisions the same way. | attempt ≤ 8 | §9.6 l.8189-8194 | §10.4; §6.3 | other: pipeline §10.4 → §10.4 / §6.3 |  |
| 398 | rule | When carrying brings two equal names into one scope, the entity whose address sorts later re-draws, and the rename is reported (§6.3); feature-conditioned names are evaluated when drawn; a successor keeps its name even where its features changed (§6.3) — features are not naming inputs. | — | §9.6 l.8194-8197 | §6.3 | other: determinism §6.3 → §6.3 |  |
| 399 | rule | Names are rerollable (rerollLayer(naming), town-wide or with ids, §7.1), editable (setAttr) and persisted; word lists are spell-checked in CI. | — | §9.6 l.8197-8198 | §7.1; §10.4 | other: pipeline §10.4 → §10.4 |  |
| 400 | test | Test (B-EDIT): a block reroll changes 0 names and 0 LabelEntity records outside its scope; placement changes outside the scope counted and reported as view changes; selecting a user corpus renames exactly the unedited names drawn from the field it replaces and no other (not the district names drawn through the same row) (§6.3; VD4-5). | 0 / 0 | §9.6 l.8198-8201 | §13 B-EDIT | rendering (§9) → §9.6 |  |

| 401 | summary | Plain-words intro restates §11: one saved file holds recipe, edits and town; every other format is a view of it; headless and embeddable (town in a background worker, finished pictures to the page); multi-user hosts keep the one true copy and generators propose changes; nothing depends on a server of ours. | — | §11 intro l.8401-8406 | — | rendering (§11) → §11.1-§11.5 | Pure restatement. |

| 402 | rule | native .plat: §2.9's format; round trip byte-identical; the canonical form; imports our own export (MFCG cannot re-import; FTG's export ≠ its edit format). | byte-identical round trip | §11.1 l.8414 | §2.9 | other: model §2.9 → §2.9 | Format owned by §2.9. |
| 403 | rule | JSON schema: published, versioned, generated from the zod types; entities by id with containment; the API's response type. | — | §11.1 l.8415 | §11.2 | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 404 | rule | Compact twin: CBOR with typed-array tags; varint zig-zag int32 cm in world coordinates, the settlement centre in the header, so it is an encoding of the same schema (FTG-21); for embeds and sharing; a decoder is part of the library. | zig-zag int32 cm | §11.1 l.8416 | — | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 405 | rule | GeoJSON (RFC 7946): two declared variants (N-14) — default strict RFC 7946 in WGS84 lon/lat through the geo anchor, and the metric file by prior arrangement; neither has a crs member; one FeatureCollection per layer; one feature per entity with id (its address, §6.3), kind, layer, epoch and public attributes; districts included (FTG omits them); ways as LineStrings with class and model width; faces as Polygons with holes, emitted without antennae (§3.6 (6)); MultiPolygons for face unions; walls, towers, gates and built bridges as typed features (a crossing site is not exported, §2.7); boundary treatments as LineStrings with treatment (VIS-8); model widths only, never symbol widths. | — | §11.1 l.8417 | §11.1 GeoJSON variants l.8426-8444; §8.2 l.7043 (model widths) | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 406 | rule | GeoJSON trees: per green entity one MultiPoint of its instance positions (§8.10 function at finest band, overrides applied; integer-cm positions under §6.5's lint so the file is byte-identical on every host, NR4-3) with species[], crownM[] and leafType[] in the same order; or one Feature per tree on request with species, crownM and leafType; or none (VN-158, XB32). | — | §11.1 l.8417 | §8.10 l.7805-7809, l.7858-7863 | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 407 | test | GeoJSON: the default file validated by an open RFC 7946 validator in CI and loaded by the scripted QGIS test (O11 d); the metric file checked for geometry validity and its sidecar against its schema. | — | §11.1 l.8417 | §13 | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 408 | rule | SVG: written from a transient display list the generation worker builds for the export (NR-5) at a declared band — city band by default; district band on request after a size estimate is shown; neighbourhood band only for a district-sized or smaller scope; <g id='layer.ground'><g id='sem.building.footprint'> groups per layer and semantic; inside a group one compound <path> per (semantic, district) and a <metadata> JSON map from subpath index ranges to entity ids; trees as one compound path per green entity (crowns at district band, wood outline at city band); halos by paint-order; hatching as compound paths; symbol widths; colour-matrix post operations as filters (§8.2). | — | §11.1 l.8418 | §8.7 l.7453; §8.9 l.7551; §8.2 post | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 409 | rule | SVG text (N-13): by default as outlines — each glyph used written once in <defs> as a path from the bundled face's own glyf outlines (quadratic segments as SVG Q), each placed glyph a <use> at S13b's per-glyph position, the label text in an aria-label and a <title>; or with text: 'embedded', editable <text> (curved labels per glyph, straight labels one <text> with explicit per-glyph x) in the bundled face embedded unmodified as an @font-face data URI (OFL permits; no subsetting, §17.2). | — | §11.1 l.8418 | §11.1 SVG text l.8446-8448; §8.9 text l.7560-7561 | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 410 | budget | SVG budgets at METROPOLIS, city band: ≤ 650 B per building (MFCG measured 410-500 B per building: 0.42-0.66 MB for 837-1 512 buildings, 6.3 MB for 15 392) plus text — outlines ≤ 64 B per placed glyph and ≤ 0.5 KB per distinct glyph, or each embedded face's base64 bytes once (≤ 0.5 MB per face) — and ≤ 60 000 elements (SCL-14, VIS-18). | ≤ 650 B/building; ≤ 64 B/glyph; ≤ 0.5 KB/distinct glyph; ≤ 0.5 MB/face; ≤ 60 000 elements | §11.1 l.8418 | §12 (budgets) | rendering (§11) → §11.1 | Could live in §12; single home needed. |
| 411 | test | SVG CI: open the METROPOLIS file in Inkscape headless (export to PNG within 120 s) and check every layer id (O11 h); the outline file's Inkscape PNG and the embedded file's Chromium PNG each match PLAT's own PNG of the same frame within the visual-regression threshold (§13.4). | ≤ 120 s | §11.1 l.8418 | §13.4; §11.1 SVG text rationale (CI renders each variant) | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 412 | rule | PNG / tiles: the band-streamed tile assembly of §8.8 at any DPI, rastered in the tile worker from the export's transient list (NR3-6); transparent background; per-layer transparent PNGs; print presets A4…A2 at 300 dpi; O12 (e): A2 at 300 dpi with no canvas larger than one tile band. | A4-A2 at 300 dpi | §11.1 l.8419 | §8.8 print; §8.7 | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 413 | rule | VTT: Foundry-class scene JSON and Universal VTT (.dd2vtt) by the profile below, one scene set per model layer (underground/ground/sky) with its elevation; grid alignment error 0 px per cell (O11 g); no manual steps on import. | 0 px per cell | §11.1 l.8420 | §8.7 VTT; §11.1 VTT profile | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 414 | rule | 3-D glTF 2.0 binary (D-8): ground as one planar partition — the plat's leaf faces at the finest committed level, each boundary read once from the arena (§3.9), each face exported once (no coplanar overlap), clipped to terrain cells and draped (vertex z by bilinear terrain sample), material by role; rivers as sloped surfaces (water level at each vertex = nearest course station's, falling monotonically downstream along S1's carved course); lakes, ponds and sea as level planes (GEO-40). | — | §11.1 l.8421 | §3.9 | rendering (§11) → §11.1 | ids D-8 |
| 415 | rule | glTF buildings extruded from baseCm (FTG-13) to eave height with roof meshes to ridge height, per wing (each wing's ring, §2.6; roof by the wing's kind as in §8.6), each wing's foundation skirt extruded down to the terrain (§2.6); walls, towers and gatehouses as prisms on their bases; bridge and pier decks at deckCm; trees as instanced crown meshes (EXT_mesh_gpu_instancing, positions from §8.10), one instancing node per (green entity, crown class) whose extras carry the entity's address and, per instance in order, species, crownM and leafType (XB32); OBJ as a flat secondary (one mesh per entity). | — | §11.1 l.8421 | §8.6 roof by wing kind; §8.4 heights | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 416 | rule | Shadows, glTF and CityJSON agree by construction (heights are model data). | — | §11.1 l.8421 | §8.4 l.7355 | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 417 | rule | CityJSON 2.0: transform { scale [0.01, 0.01, 0.01], translate } so every vertex is our integer cm (lossless); Building (LoD1.2 solid base to eave; LoD2.2 roof surfaces), WaterBody, Road (lines with class and width, sampled from the way's width profile, §2.2 rule 5), PlantCover, SolitaryVegetationObject (trees from §8.10, one per instance: a GeometryInstance of the crown class's template with species, crownDiameter, height; XB32), LandUse, OtherConstruction (walls, towers, gatehouses), Bridge, TINRelief; no referenceSystem (a local metric CRS); with a geo anchor it is recorded in root property +platGeoAnchor { latDeg, lonDeg, bearingDeg }, defined by PLAT's own CityJSON Extension and listed in extensions (DEF-5, NR-7). | scale 0.01 | §11.1 l.8422 | §11.1 CityJSON extension (below) | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 418 | test | CityJSON: validated against the CityJSON 2.0 schema in CI — the anchor-less file as it is, the anchored file with PLAT's extension schema given to the validator from the repository. | — | §11.1 l.8422 | — | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 419 | rule | CSV/statistics: the statistics record per generation with provenance (O6 d); people, buildings, plots; the corpus-calibration input. | — | §11.1 l.8423 | — | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 420 | rule | Permalinks: §6.4's format; &doc= references resolve through §11.5's DocResolver; one line for unedited towns; refused, with the .plat file or &doc= offered, when the town needs a non-shipped setting pack, host content or a non-shipped style sheet (§6.4). | — | §11.1 l.8424 | §6.4; §11.5 | other: determinism §6.4 → §6.4 | Permalink rules are §6.4's. |
| 421 | rule | GeoJSON variants (N-14): the default writes WGS84 lon/lat (RFC 7946 removed crs); a geo anchor { latDeg, lonDeg, bearingDeg } places the settlement centre and turns model north by the bearing; an export option remembered per document in view key exportAnchor (§11.3); defaults to (0°, 0°, 0°), the conventional 'no real location' point. | default (0°, 0°, 0°) | §11.1 l.8426-8430 | §11.3 exportAnchor | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 422 | algorithm-step | GeoJSON projection: each model vertex (x, y) in metres, rotated by the bearing, is a transverse Mercator easting/northing (scale 1 on the central meridian through the anchor, false origin at the anchor) inverted to WGS84 with Krüger's series to fourth order in the third flattening, evaluated with dmath (byte-identical on every host); coordinates written with 7 decimals (≈ 1 cm); exterior rings CCW, holes CW (RFC 7946 §3.1.6). | 4th order; 7 decimals | §11.1 l.8430-8434 | §8.10 rationale (7 decimals) | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 423 | number | GIS ellipsoid distances match model distances within a factor 1 + (d ÷ R)² ÷ 2 — at most 3.1 × 10⁻⁶, ≤ 5 cm, at the farthest corner of the largest frame (≈ 15.7 km from the centre at aspect 2, §12.2). | ≤ 3.1 × 10⁻⁶; ≤ 5 cm; ≈ 15.7 km | §11.1 l.8434-8436 | §12.2 frame | rendering (§11) → §11.1 | The 15.7 km depends on §12.2's largest frame at aspect 2. |
| 424 | rule | GeoJSON foreign member plat records the anchor, whether it is the default, the document id and the output version. | — | §11.1 l.8436-8438 | — | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 425 | rule | Metric GeoJSON (by prior arrangement, RFC 7946 §4): x east and y north in metres from the settlement centre with 2 decimals (the cm lattice exactly), no crs, in <name>.metric.geojson, beside a sidecar <name>.metric.json giving origin, unit, axes, document id and anchor if set. | 2 decimals | §11.1 l.8438-8441 | — | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 426 | decision | Chose a strict WGS84 default with a documented default anchor over a local metric CRS with a crs member (cannot pass an RFC 7946 validator; read by GIS as degrees); the metric file keeps the lossless path. | — | §11.1 l.8441-8444 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 427 | rule | SVG text option: the export dialog offers text: 'outlines' (default) or 'embedded'; outlines read from the same bundled face labels were measured with (§9.2); the embedded face travels unmodified (OFL-permitted; no subsetting, §17.2). | — | §11.1 l.8446-8448 | §11.1 SVG row | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 428 | decision | Chose outlines as the SVG text default over embedded text (only outlines draw the same glyphs in every consumer; embedded @font-face not reliably honoured by desktop editors — PLAUSIBLE; CI renders each variant in its target consumer; a substituted face collides) — what 'text identical on every output' (§8.9) requires. | — | §11.1 l.8449-8452 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 429 | rule | Where exports run (NR-5): every export(h, fmt) runs in the generation worker, which holds the document (§8.9); model formats (.plat, JSON schema, compact twin, GeoJSON, glTF/OBJ, CityJSON, CSV, permalinks) read the model and §8.10's instance function (Exporter.write(doc, null, opts)). | — | §11.1 l.8454-8456 | §8.9 genworker | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 430 | mechanism | SVG export builds a transient list for the export's band and frame with DisplayListBuilder.build(model, band), runs S13b there at the export stop through @plat/labels with @plat/render's ObstacleSource and RoleIndex over that list (the tile worker's code, export frame as §9.2's visible region, so §13.3's digests test the code both run), writes the file and releases the list. | ≈ 4 µs/building at city band: ≈ 21 ms CITY, ≈ 54 ms METROPOLIS target | §11.1 l.8456-8464 | §8.1 item 6; §8.9 genworker | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |
| 431 | budget | The SVG export is inside §12.7's ≤ 2 s, and its list is §12.8's export-time peak (≈ 1.9 KB per building at the city band); a district-band SVG's list is counted in the size estimate shown before writing. | ≤ 2 s; ≈ 1.9 KB/building | §11.1 l.8464-8465 | §12.7; §12.8; §8.9 l.7596 | rendering (§12) → §12.7 / §12.8 |  |
| 432 | mechanism | Raster exports (PNG, print, .dd2vtt scene image) use a transient list too (NR3-6): the generation worker builds it for the export's band and frame (including the live layers the export's layer set names, as their providers stand at the request) and transfers it with the raster-export request (§8.9); the tile worker runs S13b at the export stop over it (export frame as visible region; first work of the export class; resumable, one decision per unit), rasters the export's tiles by §8.8's band-streamed assembly (each tile a resumable job in the export class; each band's composition, read-back and encoding sliced likewise), streams encoded bytes and a per-band progress message back, and releases the list; the generation worker wraps the VTT scene around the image. | — | §11.1 l.8465-8474 | §8.1 item 6 l.6902-6905; §8.9 queue class 4; §8.9 export request row | rendering (§11) → §11.1 | OPEN NR5-1: the request must also carry the look and view state at request time (sheet, view-state switches, shadow sun, light step).; conflict C02 |
| 433 | rule | The export therefore shows the model as it stood when it was requested, whatever is edited while its bands are rastered; the export dialog shows the bands done out of the bands total. | — | §11.1 l.8474-8476 | §8.9 l.7652-7654 | rendering (§11) → §11.1 | NR5-1 extends this to the look/sun/light step, not only the model.; conflict C02 |
| 434 | decision | Chose a transient list in the generation worker over handing SVG to the tile worker (the SVG writer also reads addresses, the supersedes table, layer and entity metadata, N-4) and over keeping a second full list resident (exports are occasional; rebuilt in tens of ms). | — | §11.1 l.8476-8479 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 435 | decision | Chose a transient list for raster exports over rastering from the view's list (an A2 print takes up to ≈ 20 s, longer while the view pre-empts it; an edit would tear the picture between bands; the view's list holds finer bands only for visited districts) (NR3-6). | ≈ 20 s | §11.1 l.8479-8483 | §8.9 l.7688 | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |

| 438 | rule | A file with an anchor lists extensions { Plat: { url, version } } (url = address where each release publishes the schema beside the package); a file without an anchor (default) has neither the property nor the member and is plain CityJSON; CI validates both kinds with the extension schema supplied from the repository (no fetch); a change to the property bumps the extension's version with the exporter's output version (§6.4). | — | §11.1 l.8491-8495 | §6.4 output version | rendering (§11) → §11.1 |  |

| 440 | decision | Chose a declared extension over a sidecar as primary (keeps the anchor inside the one file a GIS user moves) and the sidecar as fallback (valid by construction). | — | §11.1 l.8500-8501 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |

(host-assigned §11.3, else the document's stored session minted on first edit, §7.4; VD2-4)}) (.plat or CBOR twin; bytes transferred in); edit(h, e \| e[]) → ApplyResult (an event may arrive with fates filled); regenerate(h, scope, 'keep'\|'newRandom', {discardEdits?}) (§7.1's Regenerate and New random); adjust(h, param, value) (§7.1's Adjust, or Re-derive from Sk for an early parameter); confirm(h, token, fates, renames?) (NF-D14); cancel(h, token); undo(h, of?) (appends an undo event for the handle's session, §7.4; of only for the owner role; a conflict lists dependent events); redo(h); query(h, q) (≤ 64 KB per page, paginated); view(h, target canvas, opts?) → ViewHandle (tiles from the tile worker); setView(v, patch); export(h, fmt, opts?) → ArrayBuffer (transferred out); report(h); subscribe(h, handler) → Unsubscribe; close(h). | query ≤ 64 KB/page | §11.2 l.8535-8552 | §14; §7.1; §7.4 | rendering (§11) → §11.2 |  |
| 448 | type | ApplyResult = ok { logHead (§2.9); changed (addresses whose records changed) } \| conflict { actions: ConflictAction[] } (§7.1 dry run) \| confirm { token; fates (first 1 000; rest by query 'fates'); renames (case-(4) renames, §6.3, §7.3 item 4; rest by query 'fates'); counts per fate; scope: EditScope (resolved) } — nothing committed until confirmed. | first 1 000 fates | §11.2 l.8553-8557 | §7.1; §7.3 item 4 | rendering (§11) → §11.2 | Result shape may also be declared in §7.1 — check determinism inventory. |
| 449 | type | ConflictAction = { kind 'moveTo'\|'unlock'\|'unassign'\|'reroute'\|'stale'\|'invalid'; target?; at?: PointCm; message }. | 6 kinds | §11.2 l.8558 | §7.1 conflict actions | other: determinism §7.1 → §7.1 | Conflict actions are §7.1's dry-run output. |
| 450 | type | Query = gazetteer {text?, kinds?, page?} \| people {type?, race?, gender?, profession?, workplace?, ageRange?, household?, district?, page?} (COV-FTG-C13) \| hit {view, x, y} \| whereIs {person, day, hour} \| relations {person, up, down} (family tree, COV-FTG-C11) \| inView {view} (features-in-view list, COV-FTG-A24; non-visual route, J-S19) \| fates {token, page} (full fate list, then renames; VD4-4). | 7 kinds | §11.2 l.8559-8565 | §10 (people, relations) | rendering (§11) → §11.2 | ids J-S19 |
| 451 | interface | @plat/api/node: the same API over worker_threads plus by-value helpers generateDocument(bp): Document and exportDocument(doc, fmt, opts?): Uint8Array for scripts and tests. | — | §11.2 l.8566-8568 | §14 | rendering (§11) → §11.2 |  |
| 452 | rule | Confirming fates (NF-D14): an action whose effective scope holds protected entities (Re-derive from Sk, Adjust of an early parameter, a regeneration over locked, pinned, edited or assigned entities, 'discard edits in scope') or whose dry run lists case-(4) renames (§6.3; VD4-4; an unlock, unassign, undo, redo or revert freeing a name among them, VD4-3) runs its dry run (§7.1) and returns confirm with the fate of every protected entity (§7.3 item 4), every listed rename and token = H128(document logHead ‖ canonical bytes of the event without its fates and renames); nothing committed. | token = H128(logHead ‖ event bytes) | §11.2 l.8571-8576 | §7.3 item 4; §7.1 | other: determinism §7.3 → §7.3 item 4 (confirmation semantics) / §11.2 (API) | Confirmation rule is editing's; §11.2 keeps the API call shape. |
| 453 | rule | confirm(h, token, fates, renames) commits the event with those fates and renames when the token is current (logHead unmoved) and the fates and renames equal the dry run's; otherwise re-runs the dry run and returns a fresh confirm; a changed list is never committed silently; resubmitting through edit with fates and renames filled is the same operation (how a host bridge carries a confirmed action, §11.3); 'discard edits in scope' is a new request (regenerate with discardEdits: true) with its own confirmation; cancel drops the token. | — | §11.2 l.8576-8582 | §11.3 bridge | other: determinism §7.3 → §7.3 item 4 / §11.2 |  |
| 454 | decision | Chose confirmation by token plus fate equality over fire-and-forget with a 'force' flag (§7.3 requires the user to see and confirm every non-kept fate; a replay computing different fates must report a mismatch). | — | §11.2 l.8582-8585 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 455 | budget | API crossings: generate — in blueprint, out handle and progress events, copy, < 64 KB and < 1 KB per event; open — document bytes, transfer; edit/regenerate/adjust/confirm/undo/redo — events or token with fates and renames in, ApplyResult out, copy, < 64 KB per batch (longer fate lists by query 'fates' pages); query — one page, copy, ≤ 64 KB; view — composed tile bitmaps and hit-test slices (§8.9), transfer, ≤ 1 MB + 64 KB per tile; export — file bytes, transfer; subscribe — ApiEvents (doc.changed, view.changed, report updates), copy, < 64 KB. | < 64 KB; < 1 KB/event; ≤ 1 MB + 64 KB/tile | §11.2 l.8587-8595 | §8.9 crossings (tile bitmaps ≤ 1 MB + ≤ 64 KB) | rendering (§11) → §11.2 |  |
| 456 | rule | The headless path runs the same worker code, and its model hash equals the browser's (text is not in the model, §9.1); ids cross this boundary as <docId>/<id> (§6.3). | — | §11.2 l.8597-8598 | §9.1; §11.1 identity l.8505 | rendering (§11) → §11.2 |  |
| 457 | rule | The derivation pool uses transferred copies, never SharedArrayBuffer (§3.3), so no embedding host needs cross-origin isolation for generation; the tile worker's optional shared columns (§8.9) remain the only use of shared memory. | — | §11.2 l.8598-8600 | §3.3; §8.9 SAB row | rendering (§11) → §11.2 |  |
| 458 | rule | Engine (§3.7, SCL-22): the product bundle has no WebAssembly dependency; if M0 triggers the kernel's pre-decided fallback (Clipper2-WASM as the single engine), WebAssembly becomes an embed requirement and a host without it receives E_ENGINE_UNAVAILABLE from generate and open. | — | §11.2 l.8600-8602 | §3.7 | other: kernel §3.7 → §3.7 | Relates to KERNEL-C01/C02 (E_ENGINE_UNAVAILABLE not in KernelError union). |

### §11.3 Host bridge (14 items)

| id | kind | statement | values | stated at | restated at | subject → home | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 459 | interface | BridgeTransport { send(msg: BridgeMessage); onMessage(handler) }; shipped WindowPostMessageTransport (iframe hosts; a fixed target origin; incoming origins checked against the embed's allowlist) and MessagePortTransport (any relay: browser-extension content script, SharedWorker, chat-bus relay; COV-FTG-N06); envelope { plat: 1, type, session, seq, logHead?, body }. | envelope version 1 | §11.3 l.8608-8611 | §14 | rendering (§11) → §11.3 |  |
| 460 | interface | Bridge vocabulary (our own terms, FTG-22): host → PLAT doc.load { bytes\|ref, role, session }, doc.apply { baseLogHead, events }, view.set { patch }, export.request { fmt, opts }, query { q }; PLAT → host ready { outputVersions, schemaVersion }, doc.changed { baseLogHead, events } (a local user's proposed events), doc.applied { logHead }, doc.rejected { reason 'stale'\|'invalid'\|'role'\|'version', currentLogHead, conflicts? }, view.changed { patch }, entity.selected { id, kind }, export.ready { bytes }, query.result. | — | §11.3 l.8612-8616 | — | rendering (§11) → §11.3 |  |
| 461 | defined-term | logHead = a 128-bit hash of (document id, edit-log length, hash of the last event), in hex (§2.9; our own term, FTG-22). | 128-bit | §11.3 l.8617 | §2.9; §11.2 ApplyResult | other: model §2.9 → §2.9 | Definition belongs to §2.9. |
| 462 | rule | Convergence: the host holds the canonical document and log; a client sends its user's edit as doc.changed against its baseLogHead; the host accepts only when that base is the current logHead (appends, advances logHead, fans doc.apply to every client incl. sender), otherwise answers doc.rejected { stale }; the client applies pending doc.apply, re-runs its events through the dry run (§7.1) and resends, or shows its user the conflicts. | — | §11.3 l.8618-8622 | §7.1 | rendering (§11) → §11.3 |  |
| 463 | rule | An action needing fate confirmation (§7.3 item 4, §11.2) is sent only after the local user confirmed, as an event carrying its fates and renames; if the state moved, the client's re-run returns a fresh confirmation instead of resending; every other client replays the event with those fates, reporting a mismatch if it computes different ones; every client applies the same events in the host's order on the same output version, so every client's model hash is the same (§6.2) — after a load mismatch, once the owner has chosen (§2.9 step 4). | — | §11.3 l.8622-8627 | §11.2 confirm; §6.2; §2.9 step 4 | rendering (§11) → §11.3 |  |
| 464 | test | Test (M3a): a host and three headless clients applying 1 000 interleaved random events — undo and redo from every client and one owner undo through a chain — converge to one model hash with 0 lost accepted events; one forced load mismatch settled by an owner's keep while two editors edit on the replayed town: all four converge to one model hash, editors' events re-applied on the saved town (VD3-4). | 3 clients; 1 000 events; 0 lost | §11.3 l.8627-8631 | §13 | rendering (§11) → §11.3 |  |
| 465 | rule | Version handshake: on doc.load the client compares the document's outputVersion (§6.4), not the generator version, with the output versions it can replay (announced in ready); when it cannot, it opens the snapshot read-only (role drops to viewer, says so) as §2.9 step 2's frozen view does — checked by its chunks' content hashes only, labelled 'made with o<k>; not verified by this version', never reported as a mismatch (VD4-1); never replays under another version silently. | — | §11.3 l.8632-8636 | §2.9 step 2; §6.4 | other: model §2.9 / determinism §6.4 → §2.9 step 2 (frozen view) / §11.3 (handshake) |  |
| 466 | rule | View state: time, location, favourites, notes and public notes, view pins, layer choices, stylePreset, season, weather, frame, rotation, the compass, legend and emblem settings and exportAnchor (§11.1) live in the document's view section outside the model hash (§2.9); each key is last-writer-wins by (Lamport counter, session id) and travels as view.set / view.changed. | LWW by (Lamport, session) | §11.3 l.8637-8640 | §2.9 view section; §8.2 season/weather; §9.5 compass/legend; §11.1 exportAnchor | rendering (§11) → §11.3 |  |
| 467 | rule | Time is the view key time: view.set { time } sets it and the module's sim.advance { hours } (a module-tier host → PLAT message) advances it under the same last-writer-wins rule and lets the simulation module recompute occupancy for the new hour; it changes no model and logs no event (FTG-22; §10.3). | — | §11.3 l.8640-8643 | §10.3 | other: pipeline §10.3 → §10.3 / §11.3 |  |
| 468 | rule | Style is canonical; the viewer's preset is not (N-15): the document's style (§2.9 style: sheet and version, row operations, ground-cover regions) changes only through logged setStyle events, carried in doc.changed / doc.apply and sent only by owner and editor roles; view key stylePreset is a per-viewer preset choice that never writes setStyle — it replaces the document's sheet for that viewer only, with the document's own rows (thematic overrides, ground-cover regions) applied where the preset has the roles they name, the rest listed in that viewer's style dialog. | — | §11.3 l.8644-8650 | §2.9 style | rendering (§11) → §11.3 |  |
| 469 | decision | Chose a separate preset key over treating the canonical style as a last-writer-wins view key (a viewer's look must neither rewrite the shared style nor silently diverge from it). | — | §11.3 l.8650-8652 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 470 | rule | Roles owner \| editor \| viewer given by doc.load; a viewer's client never emits doc.changed (edits disabled in the UI and dropped by the bridge) and emits only the view changes the host allows; the host enforces roles too; the host assigns one session per user and document and passes it on every load; a bridged client never mints a session or writes the session view key (§7.4; VD2-4). | 3 roles | §11.3 l.8653-8656 | §7.4 | rendering (§11) → §11.3 |  |
| 471 | rule | Bridged load verification (§2.9 step 3) runs while the client's user edits; on a mismatch only an owner client may keep the saved town (a rebase sent like any event); every other client takes the replayed town and reports the mismatch (NR-8); it keeps the doc.load bytes until settled; when the owner's keptSnapshot rebase arrives it installs the saved town and re-applies events after h₀ (§2.9 step 4; VD3-4); under §6.2 (iii) its root comparison with the owner's stateDigest waits for its load's recomputation (VD4-2), a still-verifying client stops, and a client loading after the owner's choice takes the saved town as base without asking. | — | §11.3 l.8656-8663 | §2.9 steps 3-4; §6.2 (iii) | other: model §2.9 → §2.9 steps 3-4 | Load-verification semantics are §2.9/§6.2's; §11.3 keeps only bridge-specific clauses. |
| 472 | rule | Logging (LC-31): every model change over the bridge is a doc.apply of EditEvents logged one to one; no bridge command changes the model any other way; undo and redo are EditEvents (§7.4) travelling as doc.changed / doc.apply, the envelope's session is their EditBase.session, and a client never truncates a bridged log; a provider output refused at §2.9's cap returns the requesting edit as doc.rejected { reason 'invalid', conflicts } (VD2-8). | — | §11.3 l.8664-8668 | §7.4; §2.9 cap; §11.4 | rendering (§11) → §11.3 |  |

### §11.4 Host inputs (3 items)

| id | kind | statement | values | stated at | restated at | subject → home | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 473 | rule | Host inputs (DEF-11) only through these doors: (1) blueprint fields (seed, rung, population, intent, character, circumstances, assignments) to generate; (2) edit events via the API or doc.apply, logged; (3) tables, presets and styles as editTable, editPreset or setStyle events whose content is copied into the document with provenance host (source, date, version), so the document replays without the host; (4) a registered ContentProvider (§14) accepted only after a double-run check (at registration it runs twice on the same input and stream; outputs must be byte-identical, else refused), every output recorded in the document's hostContent block keyed by (provider id, version, input hash) and read from there on replay. | 4 doors | §11.4 l.8674-8680 | §14 ContentProvider; §2.9 hostContent | rendering (§11) → §11.4 |  |
| 474 | rule | A provider output that would pass §2.9's 2 MB cap on pack data and host content is refused at the call, nothing is recorded, and the requesting edit returns an F-EDIT conflict naming the provider and the size (§5.2; VD2-8); so a document never needs its host to replay, and host code — which §6.5's lint cannot reach — is made deterministic by the check and the record. | 2 MB cap | §11.4 l.8680-8684 | §2.9 cap; §5.2 F-EDIT; §11.3 logging | other: model §2.9 → §2.9 (cap) / §5.2 (F-EDIT) |  |
| 475 | rule | The mapping of SettlementForge's own data onto these doors is owner-staged (after the standalone generators are mastered); this contract is its hook. | — | §11.4 l.8684-8685 | §1 scope | other: scope §1 → §1 / §16 | Staged-subject hook; allowed by the no-defer rule as roadmap-staged. |

| id | kind | statement | values | stated at | restated at | subject → home | notes |

| 476 | decision | Decision: the product contains no hosted service; for multi-client use a host (a VTT module, a web page, or SettlementForge in its owner-staged phase) is the store (§11.3) and owns accounts and access (DEF-12, COV-FTG-N07). | — | §11.5 l.8691-8692 | §1 scope (D-4, D-21) | rendering (§11) → §11.5 |  |
| 477 | interface | DocResolver { resolve(ref): Promise<ArrayBuffer>; publish?(bytes, access 'private'\|'link'\|'public'): Promise<string> }, configured by the embedding app; &doc=<ref> links produced only when a resolver with publish is configured, otherwise the app offers the .plat file and, for a small edit log, §6.4's inline compressed log; access levels are the resolver's; the reference app ships a file resolver (local files and inline logs) and no network resolver. | — | §11.5 l.8693-8696 | §11.1 permalinks; §6.4 | rendering (§11) → §11.5 |  |
| 478 | decision | Chose host-as-store over our own sync service (standalone backend-free library, D-4, D-21; the host owns accounts and storage; §11.3 makes any host a correct store; reversing = writing a service that plays the host's role, low cost). | — | §11.5 l.8697-8699 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md | ids D-21, D-4 |
| 479 | decision | Chose a transport-agnostic bridge plus the static VTT exports over a Roll20 extension (needs a Roll20 account and store publishing; FTG's extension only relays the messages MessagePortTransport carries). | — | §11.5 l.8700-8702 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |

| 484 | defined-term | Low-end device (O12 b's ≥ 30 fps clause; the low hardware tier): Intel Core i3-10110U (2 cores, 4 threads, UHD Graphics 620), 8 GB, Chrome stable — or any machine whose calibration score is within ± 15 % of it. | ± 15 % | §12.1 l.8736-8737 | §8.9 fps; §8.8 tiers | rendering (§12) → §12.1 |  |
| 485 | defined-term | Calibration benchmark (bench/calibrate, fixed inputs): 5-octave fBm over 256² cells, bucket-queue priority flood over 256², a 20 000-node Dijkstra, a 100 000-element typed-array sort, and in browser runs a Canvas2D raster of 32 fixed tiles; every score stored with the host's CPU model and core counts, memory, OS, Node version and browser name/version (UA client hints) (N-27 b). | 32 fixed tiles | §12.1 l.8738-8741 | §8.8 tier pick (Canvas2D part: 8 fixed tiles, ≈ 40 ms) | rendering (§12) → §12.1 | §8.8 says the Canvas2D part is 8 fixed tiles; §12.1 says 32 fixed tiles — see conflict.; conflict C04 |
| 486 | rule | CI divides every timing by (runner score ÷ reference score) before comparing with a budget; the reference score is stored with the goldens. | — | §12.1 l.8742-8743 | — | rendering (§12) → §12.1 |  |
| 487 | rule | Where each budget is measured (N-27 a): model budgets in Node 24 (worker_threads for the pool) on every tier and, in the nightly tier (§13.8), again in Chrome's worker on the same seeds and sizes; where the two differ by more than ± 15 %, the Chrome figure is checked against the budget and both are reported; frame, tile, first-map and label budgets in Chrome (Playwright, GPU on); p50 and p95 over B-CITY's seeds per rung and water setting. | ± 15 % | §12.1 l.8744-8748 | §13.8 | rendering (§12) → §12.1 |  |
| 488 | rule | Timing on shared runners (NR3-4): a gating CI timing (cost-model sweeps §13.2, first-map/settle/query runs §12.5/§8.9, export runs §12.7, the pool comparison and max-clause re-timing §12.3) is taken in the timing phase: last in its tier, alone on the runner, batches ≤ 60 s with bench/calibrate before and after each, > 5 % score difference re-run, pool size recorded with every timing; timings taken beside other suites (nightly B-CITY beside the invariant suite, B-FUZZ, B-EDIT, §13.8) screen and never gate; a flagged generation is re-timed in the timing phase (§12.3). | ≤ 60 s; 5 % | §12.1 l.8749-8755 | §13.8; §12.3 | rendering (§12) → §12.1 |  |
| 489 | budget | The timing phase costs ≤ 20 min of the nightly budget: sweeps and first-map runs are nine sizes × five seeds in Node and Chrome (≈ 1-2 min generating); re-timing at most 20 generations × 3 runs per rung (≤ ≈ 3.5 min at the band tops). | ≤ 20 min; 9 × 5; 20 × 3; ≤ ≈ 3.5 min | §12.1 l.8755-8757 | §13.8 nightly budget | rendering (§12) → §12.1 |  |
| 490 | decision | Chose one timing phase over a noise rule per check (runner contention inflates every gating timing the same way; a five-seed p95 is in effect a maximum). | — | §12.1 l.8757-8759 | — | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
| 491 | rule | Confirming milestones: model laws M1 (TOWN, CITY) and M2b (METROPOLIS), §12.3; first map, settle and re-raster M1 and M2b, §12.5; exports M1 (SVG, PNG) and M2b (METROPOLIS SVG), §12.7; memory M1 and M2b and the load-verification case at M2a, §12.8. | — | §12.1 l.8760-8763 | §12.3-§12.8 each; §16 | rendering (§12) → §12.1 | Index restating each subsection's milestone; drift risk. |
| 492 | rule | Budgets never change output: a missed budget is reported, never answered at run time by computing something coarser (F-BUDGET-TIME is reporting-only, GEO-2); pre-decided changes are configuration decisions taken once at a milestone and reported, and none changes the model. | — | §12.1 l.8764-8766 | §5.2 F-BUDGET-TIME; §5.1 (5) GEO-2 | rendering (§12) → §12.1 |  |

| 598 | decision | Declared trade (MFP-5, N-2, NR-1): chose a per-building cost of ≈ 58-75 µs at p95 (§12.3's laws at 1 333, 5 200, 13 460 buildings; NR3-5) over matching MFCG's 12-39 µs, because PLAT builds what MFCG does not (heightfield with hydrology, frontage-first plots with yards and party walls, site plans with heights, roster siting, greenery by rule, collision-checked labels), which D-7 ranks above speed; restates §12.5's first-map miss (≈ 14 200 buildings / ≈ 48 000 people knee; ≈ 2.3 s at the 120 000 cap; O12 (g)) and §12.3's O3 (d) miss (p95 up to ≈ 4 500 / 17 700 / 58 800 people, max up to ≈ 3 400 / 13 600 / 45 800; ≈ 0.2 / 0.48 / 1.9 s at band tops); reversing means dropping plot and site-plan detail from the one-click path, which the owner's plot-model ruling forbids. | ≈ 58-75 µs vs 12-39 µs | §12.9 l.9236-9244 | §12.3 declared; §12.5 declared; §12 intro; §15.2 | rendering (§12) → §12.3 / §12.5 (declarations) | Third statement of both declared misses (§12 intro, §12.3/§12.5, §12.9).; ids D-7 |
| 599 | decision | Chose the derivation pool as primary over a single worker: after LC-2's eager allocation the per-district stages are pure functions of eager upstream state and their district's sub-stream, so they run in parallel without shared writes; by the laws the pool keeps CITY's p95 within 300 ms up to ≈ 5 200 buildings (≈ 17 700 people) where a single worker holds it only to ≈ 3 770, and moves the METROPOLIS one-second knee from ≈ 11 400 to ≈ 14 200 buildings (§12.5); M1 and M2b keep it primary only where it measures faster with identical digests (§12.3; NR3-1). | ≈ 5 200 vs ≈ 3 770; ≈ 11 400 → ≈ 14 200 | §12.9 l.9245-9250 | §12.3 primary rule | rationale (move to docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md) → → docs/ARCHITECTURE-RATIONALE.md |  |
